Maybe enough for an email. But perhaps, infected or not, it would allow for this woman to reach out to the man she so craved and tell him whatever she thought she still needed him to know. And despite the fact that she was taking a huge risk here, that she was breaking her own standards, something told her that, when Mia would find the SIM, she wouldn’t tell anyone it was there. She was resistant. She hadn’t given up. Mia was still fighting even if she must know she was just postponing the inevitable. And for the first time she wondered if that was not all that counted. It didn’t matter what you were and where you were headed. As long as you chose to fight.

She stared at Mia for another second. Then she turned around and left, without hesitating this time. She had already lingered far too long. She exited and found the hallway still empty. She took out her gun and unlocked it regardless. She would fire her way through if necessary. All that mattered at this point was getting out. The pale light spilled through the dirty windows and stretched white rectangles across the warped floorboards. Every step she took hurt. With holding her gun ready now, her shoulder was becoming increasingly difficult to ignore. To an extent that it made her feel kind of unstable. She had no idea if she would be able to buffer recoil in her present condition. But it didn’t matter. If required, she knew she had to. As she ghosted through the hallway she noticed a deep grinding ache near her collarbone, where the brick had rested. Perhaps it was broken. But fractures healed. It didn’t matter.

She reached the staircase and paused to listen. When she didn’t hear anything, she started descending. The first few steps went well enough. The second flight nearly sent her crashing face first into the railing. Her mind started to spin, her vision blurred. For a second she thought she would fall. But her hand mercifully found the banister. She blinked away black spots that started to dance through her vision. Get it together. As she waited for the nausea to pass she calculated the time she would need to make it back to her car. Reaching this compartment had taken her about an hour. During the day. Now it was night. And she was… Well. Not in the best condition she had ever seen. So she assumed it would in the worst case double the time. But anyway. Only roughly two hours away from breathing again. There was no point in falling apart now. Only two hours. Two fucking more hours on an assignment that had felt like a whole life.

She stood perfectly still for another moment. Then pushed forward. Took one step after another, until she saw the side entrance she had used to get inside. It was within eyesight already. So close, she almost didn't believe it. But she kept moving until she heard something. A wet thud echoed from somewhere beneath the floorboards. She froze. It was followed by another, then another, directly underneath her feet. Like something was walking in the basement. Walking, but slowly. Unevenly, accompanied by a faint wet scraping that seemed to drag between the steps. Then came a low gurgling click. It didn’t sound humane. But not like an animal either. She knew these kind of sounds and had a good guess what it could be. Certainly none of the Bakers. They sounded different. They moved less predictable, less… robotic. Her heart started to hammer, her whole being clenched, wanted to fall into a panic, wanted to surrender. Was this….? One of the missing persons? Were they down there? Or what was left of them? Whatever had become of them?

She gritted her teeth. She wanted to know. She wanted to figure this place out. But she was barely holding it together. And she didn’t know how to get down there, hadn’t seen a way into the basement. And even if she found one, what happened if someone woke up? If her way outside became blocked? Now no one had seen her. Now she had the chance to fully complete her mission. It wasn’t her objective to find bodies. She had been assigned to find out what was going on and where. And collect a sample. And she had done exactly that.

She wouldn’t risk a confrontation, she decided. She wouldn’t go looking. She had to move the fuck out. Nothing else mattered, just reaching this goddamned door before her. Which she did, approximately ten seconds after. The old hinges creaked softly when she pushed it open and she flinched. But didn’t pause, didn’t look back. She closed it softly behind her as cold wind struck her face. Fresh air. It felt so intoxicating after days within these rotting walls that it gave her enough sanity to move cautiously through the shadows to reach the woodland and not mindlessly dart towards them immediately.

After a few meters she reached the swamp, mud swallowing her boots with every step. She felt so heavy. Yet she dragged her half dead body forward. The night seemed endless. Trees loomed from the darkness. Occasionally she stumbled over tangled roots and reeds, felt the pain from her shoulder nearly forcing her on her knees. But she rigidly remained upright. Because still there were small mercies. Like the clear sky that allowed for the moonlight to guide her way. Like the bar she retrieved from her pocket and devoured as she walked. Her car was somewhere out there. There was water in there. She had gone three days straight without any hydration and yet she resisted the almost inhumane urge to simply drink off the muddy water. She had no idea how far and how deeply the fungus had spread. There was no way in hell she would allow herself to risk anything at this point.

Branches tangled in the fabric of her jacket and brought her to pause. She didn’t bother trying to free it. She just shrugged it off, ignoring the striking pain it gave her and then simply cut it out. She dragged it over the ground behind her as she proceeded. She would throw it away next possible convenience anyway.

Only then, ankle deeply drowning in mud, she realized that her mission was complete. She could leave Louisiana. She could go. For a moment she squeezed her eyes shut, forced herself to not think about it. Hope was a distraction and often deceptive. She wasn’t out yet. There still were things to do. She didn’t know who Mia Winters had worked for. Who had owned the vessel. She would have to find that out first then contact her contractor. And when all this was done, only then she could start thinking ahead. Thinking of him. Thinking of the salacious taste of his skin, of….

She froze in her tracks. Stared forward. There was a faint shape between the trees. For a moment she couldn’t move, couldn’t react. And then she ran. Ran at last because now there was no holding back, because now adrenaline was elevating her. Before her was her car. Her hand shivered as she pulled the handle and clumsily opened the door. She all but dumped inside, immediately swiped the space underneath the passenger seat to retrieve the water bottle that had rolled there during her drive here. She hastily screwed it open and drank in heavy gulps. Her throat still burned, protested but she emptied the bottle resolutely.

She started the engine and it came to life. Her vision blurred, the rush that had lifted her before started to wear off rapidly. She started to shiver, turned on the seat heater. For a second she rested her head back. Okay. Get the hell back to the motel now. But she couldn’t. Her whole body trembled. And as much as she wanted to get going right away, she had to take a minute or two to regain composure. Which, all in all, turned into about two hours. Two hours of shivering and hurting and fading in and out. Eventually, however, she felt steady enough to get back out and move to the trunk to get another water. This time, it went down more smoothly. She would drive now. She would handle it. And so she went back in, fastened her seatbelt and reversed gear.

When she had reached her motel, the first thing she did was reinsert another SIM card into her cell phone. Before getting food, before taking a shower, before anything she dialed. The call connected on the fourth ring. She had full well expected it not to. "Well" he said "this is either an attempt for an apology or the years are finally catching up with me and I am getting senile." She leaned against the wall beside the bed and looked out across the scarcely occupied parking lot. "I am sorry Chase. For calling you. And… about before." She realized her voice sounded hoarse, cleared her throat. "No, you are not, Lu. I might not know much about you, about who you really are, about what you truly intend. But I know you are not acting out of a mood. You are calculated. And if you’d thought there had been anything to gain from me, you would have stayed.” He was mistaken. “You are wrong. I do need you.” For a long moment there was heavy silence. Then she heard him exhale. “Don’t bother trying to coax me again. Just give me a minute to decide whether to be offended forever." A smile briefly touched her lips. "I think you have already decided. You never struck me as the sensitive type." He huffed. “I'm not. But generally, when a woman disappears after making me fly across the globe to see her after years, I appreciate a kiss. And a courtesy fuck. Or a callback. At least." “I am calling you now. And I left you my number.” He laughed now, hard, cold, sarcastic. “Yeah. Thank you so much for it. Was almost tempted to dial.” She sighed. Having fucked him would have made things a lot easier now. And yet she hardly regretted it. No, not at all. Quite on the contrary, she realized. She was glad as hell. Her mind threatened to drift. She didn’t allow it. “Chase, there is something I need to know.” He didn’t let her finish. “The answer is yes. Oh yes, I would have done you very good. Better than anything you ever had.” She narrowed her eyes. He had not the slightest hint of an idea how wrong he was. She shifted. But. Not. Now. “Mia Winters. Ever heard of?” she asked ignoring his previous comment entirely. There was a pause during which he most likely debated with himself whether to simply disconnect the line. She was thus slightly surprised when he answered instead. "In this regard I might have to disappoint you. I've never heard the name before in my life." He didn’t sound like he was lying. “Okay. What about Alan Droney?" “Neither. Would that be all?” “Almost. F Foundation?”

This time a long silence followed. When he spoke again, the humor had faded. "Now that's interesting." She held her breath. “You know the company." He sighed deeply at the other end of the line. "I know of it." "From where? Who does it operate for?” There was another pause. She could almost hear him considering how much to say. Then he sighed. “Lu. I told you to walk away.” She raised a brow. “I was never particularly good in following orders.” He hummed lowly. “And I cannot deny that it is one of the things I find deeply tempting about you” he commented, now mildly amused. But then his tone changed. “You already know my answer to your question.” "Humor me" she whispered. “I had every intention to. But you decided to leave.” It was neither confirmation nor proof but everything she needed to know. F Foundation belonged to The Connections. So they had sent the vessel to transport E-001 to it’s destination in South America. Mia Winters and Alan Droney had accompanied the ship and it’s cargo. It had gotten into a storm then, had undergone shipwreck. Mia Winters and the cargo had been taken in by the Bakers. From what it looked like, the pictures in the house, the way they had spoken, behaved, it seemed likely that they had simply intended to help. And then all of them had gotten infected by the subject. They had done something to the people that had gone missing. Only she didn’t know what. Perhaps she could somehow find it out. But not now. What she saw confirmed now though was that somehow, her contractor was involved in all this. She assumed he worked for the Connections as well, that they or parts of them had lost track of the cargo and sent her to retrieve it. Under the radar. Without causing a stir. She decided she would email her contact next.

Chase filled the silence once more. "Don’t you think a thank you would be in order for my cooperation?” She didn’t hesitate. “Thank you, Chase.” She meant it. She was grateful. Another silence settled between them. But this time, it somehow didn’t feel uncomfortable. And eventually Chase chuckled. "I was thinking of a lot more than that, actually. But as it seems, this is all I will get at present. You really have a talent for making a man feel used." Despite the absurdity of the moment, she laughed. "Your ego will recover." "I am optimistic" his voice softened slightly "though for the record, if you ever decide you'd like to pick up where we left off... You know how to reach me.” Her smile was still in place. A lesser man would have sounded hopeful. Chase sounded amused. Confident enough to let the moment end on his expense.

"Goodbye, Chase." "Until next time, Lu. Take care of yourself while chasing the shadows. Don’t let them swallow you up.” Then the line was disconnected and she immediately took out her laptop. She logged onto an encrypted platform and left her contract from Miami a message. Objective completed. Awaiting debrief instructions.

The response had come without delay. Report back immediately. Name location and time. She chose Montreal. Not the USA, entirely neutral and unconnected to her but close enough to home.

She arrived first in a small café she had once visited. It had been the only time she had been to the city altogether. But she had never forgotten it. It was nice, located in a side street of Old Montreal, a place with dim lightning and dark wooden furniture. It was busy mid- afternoon. Crowded to a degree that nobody would remember faces and still with tables set privately enough to allow conversations. She sat at a corner table near the windows and ordered herself a coffee. And it tasted good. Perhaps a bit too good, a bit too normal, a contrast slightly too sharp against the previous days. Not long ago she had thought about letting go. And now, barely 48 hours later she was sitting in this lively spot, wearing clothes she had just bought, enjoying a doppio. She hadn’t allowed herself a moment’s rest, had slept only a little, had functioned like a machine, hadn’t gotten her injuries examined. Following her return she had found herself something to eat, had slept then and afterwards had started putting together her report. It had taken her most of the subsequent night after which she had booked herself a flight around noon.

Her contact entered after a while, scanning the place immediately. She looked away, didn’t bother making her presence known in any way, watched the people outside pass by instead. Tourists with cameras, business people carrying coffees. A couple kissing beside a halting bus. As if nothing had happened. As if nothing was going on, as if there were no other concerns than answering emails timely or seeing every site there was in order to post the exact same picture millions of others already had on Instagram. The chair beside her got pulled back. She didn’t look.

“Good to see you again Ms. Chou. I hope you had a pleasant journey here. A beautiful city to visit.” She glanced in his direction. “Thank you.” Without missing a beat, she reached into her bag and took out an envelope. She put it on the table between them. Met his eyes.

“Everything is in there. Copies of files. My summary. The sample.” The man looked content. “I assume this is the only documentation on the assignment that exists.” She nodded slowly. “Yes. I put copies of the photos I took on the stick inside. The phone I used for it is erased and destroyed. There is no data left.” At that a cold smile spread on his lips. “Very well. I am interested in your personal impression. What is going on?” She now steadily surveyed him. Didn’t break eye contact as she spoke.

“I followed the vessel first.” He didn’t tense, didn’t blink, didn’t react at all. “I learned that two people were assigned to accompany a cargo, E-001, to a destination in South America. The ship was wrecked by a storm and the cargo escaped containment and killed one of the agents. The other agent I assume got washed up at a shore somewhere near the location where I was able to track her down. And the cargo.

The cargo seems to be a bioweapon, it was engineered. I wasn’t able to determine it’s origin. But I was able to find it’s current habitat. It has spread vastly inside a ground owned by a family named Baker. I encountered three family members. Jack Baker and Lucas Baker. A female, Jacks age, perhaps Marguerite. They all seem responsive, able to interact, DNA intact. From a memo, however, I learned that it might be likely that E-001 has infected them.

Also, I saw Mia Winters, the surviving agent. She is infected, too. As ordered, I did not interact with anyone and was able to pass without being uncovered. I was not able to find out what has happened to the civilian missing’s. But I strongly believe that they are there somewhere. Or… Were. Something is going on in the basement of the house. There are… subjects there. But I don’t know if it’s the missing’s or something else. From the sound of it I would assume nothing that is still humane at least.”

A faint smile had meanwhile spread on his thin lips. “Very impressive work indeed. How long did it take for you to get to the bottom of it?” She arched a brow. “After we met in Miami, I immediately took off.” Slowly he nodded. “Do you think anyone else who had your leads would have been able to follow them? Or did you have help?” She furrowed her brows. “I operate alone if that is what you are asking.” She knew it was not. He was evaluating the chances of someone else being able to find what she had found now that he knew it had been possible.

She decided to dare him a little. “But if you are rather worrying where the BSAA stands in this I can assure you that currently, they are inactive.” He stared back at her. “And how can we be sure?” She simply held his gaze. “I checked for myself.” “How that?” “I don’t elaborate on my sources.” His eyes narrowed. “Fair.” She drew in a deep breath. “You know what I wonder, though?” she asked, pretending to be asking casually. “Why hasn’t the owner of that ship gone looking? It was… well… valuable cargo. And another thing. You ask me about the BSAA but only if I was sure they were on standby. Not what I was thinking why that was the case.”

His countenance remained blank but something in his eyes flickered. Perhaps for just an instant. Then he offered another smile. “You are very observant, Ms. Chou. I am impressed by you, I cannot deny it, even if your reputation preceded you.” She kept her expression hard, didn’t answer. “So… Now you mentioned it I am intrigued. What do you think then? Why does the BSAA not act?” For a long moment she just stared back unblinking. There were two options now. Dare him further and risk making him uncomfortable and nervous and therewith a potential threat for her or stay on the safe side but not get anything out of him and live with the uncertainty. The choice didn’t feel like one.

“I have a theory” she started, leaning forward, eyes piercing straight into his now. “Perhaps the BSAA had leads but lacked respective orders to also take them seriously. And, huh, how very lucky for us, they also seem to be distracted. By internal quarrels. By an unknown potential antagonist. That, in my eyes, might just be what it pretends to be. And that, yet, serves so well as a scape goat. Something so visible, a shiny ray of despise, so bright that it overshadows everything that lingers in the darkness around it.”

She saw his jaw tighten. And smiled herself now. “But pursuing this wasn’t my objective, was it? So who am I to ask questions.” She crossed her arms, leaned back, knew he was thinking, he couldn’t hide it. “These are very interesting observations, Ms. Chou. But I am afraid I have no answers for you. Still something to offer.” She pushed her brows skywards. “And what would that be?” “In my line of work we value success and discretion and capability. You offer all of these qualities. I have two or three things more that I would like you to take care of. Renumeration will be generous. And I would like you to meet some executives within my organization. You offer a high potential for a more permanent cooperation.” My organization.

His organization that was a player in the field not she, not the BSAA, nor DSO had even been remotely aware of. They were active and operating globally. They were powerful, seemed to reach into politics and governmental installments. She knew nothing about them but Chase had indicated that they had no face, no public image, not a singular executive management. It was a network, perhaps impossible to track down. And she had the lead now. He was extending her a way in. All she had to say was yes. To this most likely unique chance of getting ahead of things, of finding out more.

She looked at him again. “I will consider it.” He opened his mouth, but she raised a finger to indicate to him that she had accidentally forgotten to mention something when it really had been intention. “Oh, almost forgot. What are you planning now? Are you extracting the subject? Retrieve Mia Winters?” He stared right back at her, choosing silence. She smiled faintly. Hadn’t expected anything else. After another moment he rose and picked up the envelope. She just remained. He looked down at her. “Get back to me when you are ready for you next assignment.” She raised her eyes, tilted her head faintly in acknowledgement. Didn’t say goodbye, didn’t agree either.

An hour later she sat in the rental car and merged into traffic. She didn’t think as she left Montreal behind her. She pointed the vehicle toward autoroute 10 leading south. Towards the United States border.

A few hours later the skyline appeared in the distance, glass and steel rising beyond trees, growing larger with every mile and illuminating the black sky above. The sheer shimmering of the city lights looked unreal, like a dream. Tempting but untouchable once you got close. She left the interstate to fill into the stream of local traffic. A green sign pointed east toward downtown. She ignored it. Instead, sharply turned into a parking lot of a roadside motel. She killed the engine and got out.

She walked to a bench. The effect of her painkillers wore off and she had to sit down for a minute. And for the first time since weeks, months, she paused. Really paused. There was nothing she needed to do right now. There was no schedule. And yet it wasn’t over. The Connections were out there. They were working. They were developing. They were in complete darkness, a predator lurking in the shadows, out for a kill, no one had yet taken notice of. She stared at a large commercial screen across the road, watched the lights flicker as they changed their subject. She wasn’t able to pick up what they were about. She couldn’t really look, couldn’t do anything.

She longed for him so much that it hurt and now, finally, he wasn’t far anymore. Just perhaps half an hour ride to reach him after twelve weeks and three days and about thirteen hours without contact. But she just couldn’t move. Because as exhaustion replaced the previous drive in her blood in the aftermath of her desperate escape, a tightness in her bones settled in that she had not felt in a long time. She suddenly started to shiver, pressed her hands under her thighs to stop them from trembling.

It wasn’t like the feeling was unfamiliar to her. Rather it had for the longest time been second nature. She had always been afraid when she had been young. But it had stopped at some point so long ago that the memory of it had gone soft, had deceived her, had made her think that it had vanished entirely.

Just to strike back full force now. It wasn’t the things she had seen. She had seen a lot and it didn’t scare her. But the fact that only now truly settled in, as she was crushingly looking forward to see him, that it could have been different. Chance had decided that she was making it out instead of rotting away, hidden in a hole in a wall of an infected house. And he would have kept going through with his life, coming home occasionally to find the apartment still empty. Without knowing where she was. Without knowing she was dead. Or consumed by a growth she did not fully understand. Until one day he would perhaps have given up hoping entirely. Or ended up in the same house. And not find her or find a dead woman walking. And she couldn’t deal with this. It made her so deeply afraid, painfully aware.

Or, perhaps, she would now be the one to come home and find the place empty. Perhaps she would reach out to Sherry at some point to learn that one of his assignments had kept him. Or that he had been infected beyond cure. Or that he had become lost, somewhere. And even if they were alive now, what was she dragging home to him? Another organization on her heels. Another open battle for him to fight. Perhaps against the worst opponent that he’d ever had.

All of these thoughts in her head gave her a shattering feeling of incapability, of powerlessness, of being paralyzed. A feeling she had run from for all her life. A feeling she had decided she just wouldn’t allow anymore. That she couldn’t have, that she would prevent at all cost.

But now it was crushing her under. And she didn’t know how to deal with it, other than to run. Other than to cut ties with the only thing she truly cared about on earth. Because there was no way out. Even if she herself was willing to let go from here, which she didn’t really know either, she knew he wouldn’t. Couldn’t. He would take this fight on once it reached him. Not for the DSO, perhaps he would in fact actually be willing to quit if she demanded it. But he would not quit on the lives he left in danger. Because what he did wasn’t about the fucking big picture, he wasn’t like Chris. What he did was to save civilians. He would always throw himself between a bullet and someone else right in front of him. He was driven by the desire to make a difference, even if it was only for one person's life. He kept pushing through. She couldn’t protect him and he couldn’t protect her. She didn’t even know what side she was on in this game. She had served Albert Wesker and Derek Simmons, men who called themselves visionaries, patriots. Every one of them had spoken about necessity. About sacrifice. About the greater good. A greater good she had never seen. She had just walked alongside these men, watched, anticipated. Survived, taken profit from it.

It hadn’t happened to stop them. Even though she often had quietly tipped the scales when nobody had been watching to prevent things from getting uglier. It had been the only morality she’d ever allowed for herself. Saving Leon didn’t count, it had happened for selfish reasons. And now… Lines were blurring. She was changing. Deep down inside, during her mission or maybe even before, she had been starting to think about battle. She was braced for it, when she listened to herself. When she thought about The Connections she saw an enemy rather than an opportunity. And she didn’t know how to handle this. Was she taking sides now after all? Would she loose herself? Who was she anyway? She had no idea.

And she realized, as much as she wanted it, she couldn’t go to him. She didn’t know what to say to him at this point. She was spiraling deeper and deeper into her own mess. She felt her body tremble, felt her hands icy cold. And she couldn’t bring herself to move. She couldn’t stay and couldn’t go.

At some point she felt tears run down her face, felt her composure slipping. She glanced around, eyes flickering to the screen before her once more and stood to walk to the small front office of the motel. She checked in and entered one of the rooms with no luggage, nothing except what she had on her. Much like usual, perhaps a soothing familiarity when everything else seemed to fall apart around her. She didn’t switch the light on. She just sat down on the edge of the bed to stare at the wall. The room was cool, too much so. It smelled like something she couldn’t quite put her finger on. Maybe the detergent used on the sheets or a room freshener in the air conditioning. Anyway, it was repulsive. Repulsive like the rotten smell in the Baker’s house, just in a different way.

She knew their place would be warm now, it always was. It would smell like coffee and like him. It wouldn’t be dark there, like here. He always kept that little lamp on for her, when she was away. And she did the same for him. This haven was so close yet unreachable far. She took off her clothes, went to the small bathroom, hit on the shower and sat on the tiles underneath it. Let the water mix with her tears that were now running like waterfalls until they blurred into one another.

Later, in bed, she still shivered, found herself unable to find any sleep. It was entirely unlike her. She had always been able to sleep. While he had not. Regularly sleeping with him had revealed to her that he was permanently haunted by nightmares. Sometimes to an extent where his shivers and tremors woke her up. One night, when it had been particularly bad, he had cried, hadn’t stopped shivering wildly. She hadn’t really been able to soothe him then, he had not been able to calm down and find back to sleep. Until she had gone and fetched her gun, had loaded it and released the safety, had sat with her back against the headboard, had pulled him to her, held on tight and told him she would watch over him, that nothing could happen, that he was safe. And she had kept her promise. She had just sat there, held him and protected him. And he had slept until noon.

And now it was her who would have needed something to hold on to. Or perhaps rather exactly the contrary. Fuck. Her thoughts kept circling, tightening, turning in on themselves, looking for a way out and finding none. What was she supposed to do? Keep working for The Connections? To infiltrate them? Or just to learn who they were, what they knew? She had the possibility. The invitation inside. Why not utilize it?

She had always been the one who had known before anyone else. She had been in Raccoon before him, in Spain, in the Eastern Slavic Republic, had learned of Carlas plans before all of them or even as the only one… And now. She’d always kept thinking ahead. And with that often been able to protect him. Because the DSO didn’t think ahead. The BSAA didn’t think ahead. They were capable organizations with capable personnel, without a doubt. But the way they operated was too much bound to legal frameworks, too much above water. They didn’t have much choice than to focus on reaction. On damage control.

But if she did, if she accepted the next assignment, where would that put her? Was she still doing it for herself or was her reason for considering it rather something else? Would she make his battle hers? She pressed her eyes shut harder, as if that could make it stop. It was unbearable. The conflict, the indecision. And all she’d ever done to deal with things, with excessive demands, with threats, had been running. She had cut losses before they cut her. But fuck this, this was past any control, this loss couldn’t be cut because she felt it to her chilled bones.

Because he’d be alive somewhere. Unaware that she was still by his side, in the shadows. Like she had always been. Perhaps then with a bitter heart that would never forgive her but still doing what he did. Still putting himself in danger. He would keep going like Mia Winters. He would scream at nights, perhaps her name. Perhaps at some point no longer. But he would keep going. So would she be able to protect him? Even if she did think ahead. How could she make sure he stayed safe and at the same time avoid crossing paths with him again? At least until this was finished. But how did she picture finished exactly? Bringing down The Connections? She was just one person. Hardly in the place to do so, was she? But perhaps she would find a way… Perhaps not… Maybe someday… Maybe then she could… The realization hit and for a moment she just listened to her own breathing. It felt like her mind was slowing, finally some of the fog starting to clear. It was now like it had always been. She had stayed away from him to keep him safe for years. To remain ahead of things, to stop things before they got to him. And yet she had decided to stop. Not even a year ago. She’d decided to try with him regardless. Did this mean it had not worked out at this point? Did this prove that everything she, they had hoped for couldn’t be achieved? She realized that thinking these thoughts tore her heart in pieces. It caused physical pain. It made her want to shout in defiance. She gritted her teeth. She was at the same point where she’d always stood with him. It never fucking stopped.

As if running would change anything now. When it hadn’t for ages. If anything, she now considered, it would perhaps make it even worse. Because running wouldn’t remove the danger nor the fucking paralyzing numbing angst for him nor her indecision. But would remove the only thing that maybe would make it bearable. Like hearing his voice on the phone. Like seeing him walk through the door, alive, intact. Quitting only meant facing it alone. For her. And for him.

And she had told him she wanted to stop running, had told herself. And she had meant it. Just never until now fully anticipated how damn hard it was. How damn hard it was to shake everything that had shaped you in life off and surrender it to become something else. Old habits died in fact very hard.

So the fear, the uncertainties, they were perhaps simply a consequence of loving him. And what was the point of loving someone who couldn’t be kept safe, when she couldn’t even tell if she was on the same side as him? Well, love had no point. The only point was that there was no version how things could go from here in which he wouldn’t be in danger. With or without her. And she realized that from this, she perhaps actually couldn’t run. Because even if she walked away, she’d still want to be with him. She’d still desire him, she’d still want him all for herself. Distance had never managed to dilute it. Had never weakened it.

She blinked her eyes open. It was still dark around her. Perhaps a little less cold she had turned the heater on before. Perhaps a little less impenetrable because her eyes had meanwhile adjusted. She set up, ran her hand over her face. This didn’t feel like a refuge anymore. It was a delay. So what the fuck was she still doing here?

She got up, put back on her clothes, making a mental note to throw them away once she got home. Home. Half an hour now. She swung the door behind her shut with force.

She inserted her key into the lock and turned it carefully. It was the middle of the night. Everything was quiet. As she pushed the door open, she reminded herself that there was a non-zero chance that he wouldn’t be here. She had no idea what he had been up to lately. Perhaps he was in DC. Maybe even further away. She prepared herself for the disappointment, thought perhaps it would be for the better, a taste of bitterness reaching her tongue. But as she opened, she felt the tension of months ease already, momentarily, all by itself, at the comfort coming from finally being back. Back here, back at the only place she’d ever called home.

She entered and turned to softly close the door behind her. For a moment she stilled, hand still on the handle, breath shallow. It was warm here. It smelled good. It smelled… Musky, woody, with a touch of vetiver. Like him. And the scent tore on her heart, urgently, even more forcefully than she had anticipated, her eyes went dry, burned, she felt entirely overwhelmed, feared she was going to cry. From relief, from exhaustion, from pain. She wasn’t sure. She suddenly neither had the faintest idea about how to deal with this… this…this crushing relief, this confusion, this… She broke off mid thought, didn’t have time to contemplate, couldn’t catch up with her feelings because she heard something, swirled around and spotted him a second later. The sight caused her to stumble backwards against the door.

He stood in the kitchen, half-leaning against the counter, in a posture that looked like standing upright had become too much. The dim light didn’t do him any favors either. He was cast in shadow, surrounded by darkness. Within fractures of seconds she took him in, frozen to the spot. His face. Almost unrecognizable. There was a swelling around his eye, already gone dark, spreading across his cheekbone. There were cuts, jagged, uneven, across his arms, one dragging down from his collarbone into his shirt. Other wounds seemed to be underneath. The fabric was dirty, laced with bloody spots.

And then he suddenly looked up and she ceased breathing. Their gazes met. He went rigid, for an eternity just stared like he didn’t trust his own eyes. Like his brain couldn’t make sense of what he was seeing. His jaw dropped open, but no word came out. She couldn’t snap out of it either, remained with her back glued against the door behind her. She couldn’t sort out the mess she felt. The relief to find him here, the disbelief how much it hurt, the emotions raging inside of her about what had happened.

“…A- ..Ada” he stuttered and immediately trailed off, swallowed hard. As if he hadn’t been sure if he’d ever see her again. As if he knew what she had been through, as if he knew what it meant for her to stand here right now. It made her move without thinking, at last, acting on an instinct as old as the earth. She closed the distance, he turned to her. But she stopped short right before him, hesitated, didn’t even know why. His expression was still all disbelief and she had no idea what to say, what to do, how come back to him, how to come back to this. How did one return from a chapter that wasn’t closed? How did one come back before having sorted everything out? How did one come back home at all?

She lowered her gaze in indecision, looked at his arms that waveringly hung to his sides. He was bleeding, she realized and cautiously dared to reach.

She didn’t look at him when she took his wrist to turn it, to see, when she traced the scabs in his skin with her fingertips. She watched the blonde hair on his forearm rise at the touch, felt her own skin contract alike. She checked all his injuries there. It was the only thing she presently knew how to. Check his wounds. Assess the damage.

“What happened?” she whispered, her voice betraying her as she lifted her chin to take a proper look at his black eye, still avoiding his gaze. It had cracked underneath his lid, looked like it would have required stitching and would leave behind a scar. She gently touched it with her thumb and he flinched. Not hard though, not enough to draw him away from her. “Work” he muttered and she could hear in the low, rough sound of his voice how off guard he still was, how beside himself. “Just got back an hour ago or so.”

Her eyes traveled over his collar bone, his neck, down his bloody shirt. How could they have let him go like that? How had they not insisted on him having this taken care of? Anger rose, making her voice sound accusive. “You belong in a hospital, Leon. You should have gotten these treated.”

He didn’t even look, she felt his eyes firmly resting on her. She slightly shook her head, didn’t know what to do. The mess before her, the mess she felt inside. So wanted so much and nothing at all. To take him, to feel him, to push him off her, to run away. He brought his hands up. Very slowly. She would have wanted to evade, to dodge, but couldn’t move, just couldn’t react, couldn’t do anything. And then he reached and cupped her face between his palms. She fully tensed as his familiar warmth bled into her skin, as electricity pulsed through her veins. So violently that for a moment she closed her eyes. She could feel his unwavering stare. Until she slowly blinked her eyes open and was met by crystal blue eyes underneath thick lashes. “You’re back” he husked, voice breaking. She swallowed, allowing him to hold her in place. A lump in her throat suddenly made it hard to speak so she tried to nod despite his grip on her.

She averted her gaze, because she couldn’t stand that look, couldn’t believe that he was here, holding her, barely held together but so firm nevertheless. She moved her hand to his collarbone, just to do something, distract herself, carefully pulling the fabric back. A little blood had dried into it she realized and slowed, became more careful, easing it away without reopening the wound. He watched her the whole time, not letting go. He let her inspect him before he spoke again, very softly, barely above a breath. “Hey Ada. Look at me.” She didn’t answer, focused instead on the deep cuts that ran down to his chest. “Are you ok?” His grip on her tightened, he lifted her chin to force her to meet his eyes at last. She did so, after a beat, reluctantly. “Yeah.”

He searched her gaze and it felt intrusive now, she feared he would read straight to her soul. She felt her eyes burn, heaviness pulling at her features, threatening to shatter away the schooled neutrality. She tried to look away but this time he didn’t let her. She blinked, her hand on him slipping slightly. Enough for him to notice. Enough for this big hard man underneath these thick hard muscles to turn all soft and undone.

“Ada…” “I am fine” she hissed. She tried to turn her head, like she could avoid it, like she could keep this contained. When she knew he had already seen. He didn’t let go. There was no escaping him. “No. You’re not.” Said in this steady calm voice that chased away the worst demons. “It’s just…” she broke off because fuck, her voice was failing her. And she wasn’t accustomed to failure. Especially not before another person. “I hate to see you injured” she said, swallowing, trying for composure and failing miserably for the first time. Perhaps these blue eyes, this depth in them. His warmth after all the coldness before. It ripped her apart.

He gave her a look that told her he knew that it wasn’t it, expression a mix of confusion, desperation and… a deep longing. Her hands stilled completely. Because he was not wrong. She hated to find him this way but it wasn’t the problem.

“I don’t know what I am supposed to do” she admitted. It broke out of her, coming out flat and wrong and misplaced and too direct and she had no fucking clue why she told him at all when she could have remained silent instead. Maybe because there had been just silence for so long. Maybe because he was the one person she had ever talked to. Maybe because he was here now, solidly holding on to her. “There was…” she exhaled, trying to steady her voice without succeeding “…there was a moment. And I didn’t know…I wanted to…” Her voice faltered. She glanced at him and he looked back, didn’t interrupt her. Didn’t rescue her by playing it down this time. Didn’t make a joke about her inarticulation to lift the heaviness. He just let her talk while he held on. She hated him for it.

She gritted her teeth. Thought if he asked for it so stubbornly he deserved it. To feel all the pain, the uncertainties she felt. “I was thinking about you” she said like it had been an accusation. “I have considered not coming back.” Her hand dropped from his shoulder entirely. Her eyes narrowed. “You wouldn’t have known anything. And it would have been for the better.” Her composure cracked fully now, no way to hide it. Her breath hitched, her shoulders tightened as she physically tried to hold it in. Unsuccessfully. Frustration turned into aggression.

“You’d just keep going. You’d come back here and I’d just be gone.” His jaw clenched slightly, something shifted behind his eyes. “I barely made it back” she threw at him. “But it isn’t even that what bothers me. But what I might bring back to you, into what I could be pulling you into now that I returned. I don’t know if we are safe. If we will ever be. I don’t know any longer where I stand in all this. What my next steps will be. And… It makes me scared. And I am not usually scared. It is all because of you… this” she gestured vaguely with her hand as her voice fully broke. In a way she couldn’t recover from fast enough. A tear escaped.

He pulled her against him. Not gently. Not carefully. She stumbled forward, thrown off balance, and found herself with his arms around her like it wasn’t even a question. Her hands came up instinctively, gripping into the shirt at his back, to fight him off, or for something solid to hold onto, she wasn’t sure.

“We will handle it” he said, low, steady, full of conviction right against her temple. “You came back and I’m right here. And now I am not afraid anymore. And whatever it is that scares you, we will figure it out. I’ll protect you.” She wanted to believe him. She wanted to believe it was true. But it didn’t fix it. Because she knew he was lying and that deep down inside she was convinced he knew it too. “I don’t know what to do with everything” she admitted into his shoulder, hiding her face from his sight. “It never ends, Leon. This game, these battles… There is no end to it.” She bit her lip in regret. “And… I don’t know how to handle it, not anymore. Because everything has changed. I have changed. I didn’t answer that question of yours back in the days because I couldn’t because I didn’t know. But now I do. And I have no idea how to adjust, don’t know the direction from here.” She hardly ever confessed weaknesses but in this moment she couldn’t not. Letting her guard down before him had become so terrifyingly easy.

“You don’t have to” he whispered against her hairline. She could feel his heartbeat, steady, perhaps a little faster than usual. But strong. Utterly calm. She realized how close she had come to never feeling it again. She shook her head slightly, slid her hands up, allowed herself to dig her nails into the fabric of his shirt and the skin over his shoulder blades underneath, holding on tighter than she assumed was comfortable for him with all these bruises. Maybe even without. He didn’t waver. She pulled her head from his shoulder to finally look at him straight.

“Leon, I… When I thought about the possibility of not surviving … Not coming back to….you….” She tightened her grip on him still. Knew she was lost. She would be going to hell at some point. Of that she was convinced. But she wouldn’t let it end like Mia Winters. She would be brave and say what she needed to say as long as she still could. She drew in a deep breath, knew she was crossing every boundary she’d ever had with this but decided to rather do it than not to have him know at all.

“I love you.”

The world stopped turning. There was no sound, just her words resonating in the darkness. He went completely still. Not a flinch. Not a breath. After a long moment, his hold tightened on her hips. It felt like he was holding her for support now rather than comfort. “You’ve got one hell of a way of taking the long road to get where you’re going” he muttered nodding absentmindedly. And there was no humor in it. She shook her head against him, forehead back against his shoulder, done at this point. She had nothing more to say. She had already said too much. His grip on her tightened further and it felt like the weight of her words was settling in. She had never said it before. To anyone.

He drew back to look at her. She tried to avoid it, tried to step back up to him. But he didn’t allow it and so she let him pick up her gaze after all. “Say it again.” Whispered with wide moist eyes full of awe and so full of need that she didn’t hesitate.

“I love you.” His breath left him like his throat was so tight that he was in danger of choking from it. His hands lost their firmness, trembled. He was shattering, something within him was falling apart completely. Leon Kennedy was at loss for words. And that was certainly a moment to remember. He glanced sidewards, shook his head once before he centered his eyes back on hers. He gazed at her lips, leaned forward. So uncertain, so cautious, as if he had never kissed her before, as if any sudden moves would make her take back what she had just said. She caught his chin between her fingers, brushed her thumb over his jaw. He closed his eyes. And, looking at his face before her, finally there, she realized that he was the only thing that would ever truly matter in her life.

She cupped his face with her hands, kissed him softly. Lips first, then his cheeks, the black under his eyes, the crack in his brow, his forehead. Until his eyes flickered back open. And she pulled him into a full kiss. His tongue met hers and he tasted like peppermint gums and himself. He tasted like her favorite flavor. As good as he smelled. The tip of his tongue collided with hers, constantly coming back for more, heads adjusting to redefine the angle each time. Until she gently pushed him back. He looked disappointed now. It made her smile. From the heart. For the first time in weeks.

“Let me take care of you, Leon.” He leaned back in, claimed back her lips, before he muttered against her mouth “…you already are.” She kissed him back for a moment longer, unable to stop but then composed and drew away a few inches. His breath was shallow, his wet lips were still parted. It made her heart beat. Wildly. “I mean it. You are bleeding. I’ll patch you up.” Slowly, she reached for his hand, took it in hers. It felt so beautiful she almost trembled. But she didn’t let it show. Started walking and guided him along, upstairs into the bathroom.

He sat down on the rim of the tub and she carefully removed the shirt. Muscles flexed with his motion and for an instant she watched. He was so beautiful. Her memory hadn’t done him justice. She softly brushed her hand through his hair and he closed his eyes. She repeated it, kissed his forehead before she collected the first aid supplies from the drawer. She felt his gaze bore into her back.

When she returned he looked up at her. With these eyes. And she couldn’t help but smile softly, was pulled to him once she was close enough, arms locking around her waist, forehead pressing into her belly. She felt it in every fiber of her being, this embrace, his surrender, his need, the way her body reacted to it. She needed him too, tangled her fingers back into his hair to hold him in place. For a moment they remained like this, before she started to stoke him gently, whispered. “Come on. Let me see.” He let out a deep sigh and released her.

There were bruises blooming across his abdomen in deep shades of purple and blue. But the skin over them was intact. “Turn around” she told him and he obeyed. There was a long scrape stretching from his shoulder blade to his lower back. Deep cuts at the base of his spine. “How did that happen” she asked as she applied antiseptic to a swab. He huffed. “Long story. There is this rookie in my team since recently. And he’s… Well. A full-time job.” She smiled, remembered him running around in the RPD. A little of her previous anger towards the DSO dissolved instantly.

She treated him with tender hands, pressed lingering kisses to his shoulders to soothe the pain as she dabbed antiseptic on the cuts in his lower back. Then she put dressings onto every little injury. Neatly smoothing them down with her thumb. She realized how much she wanted to touch him. As much as he leaned into it. She started stroking his back, his shoulders, his neckline, his spine. And he let it happen. Let her rest her head to his nape after. She reached her around him and closed her eyes as she felt his hands come over hers. She breathed with him. And let go. She was safe. So sheltered that she realized a second too late that her vision had started to blur, that dizziness was seizing her. She lost her balance, expected her body to hit the floor any second. But she never felt the fall.

Chapter 8: North

He turned faster than his brain was able to put up with and caught her the fracture of a second before her head would have hit the ground. He supported her full weight, her body completely gone limp. He groaned as he carefully sank onto the bathroom floor with her, his shoulders shielding her from any impact. Then he pulled her up, into his lap, his own condition making it unnecessarily hard. Her head rolled against his chest as he managed to gather her in his arms. His fingers searched her neck, feeling for her pulse. It seemed regular. And she was breathing evenly.

He brushed her hair from her forehead. “Ada?” he whispered huskily. Didn’t receive an answer. He sighed deeply. She had passed out entirely. He checked her body for an indication for her condition but at first glance found none. What had happened? He briefly closed his eyes, shook his head. He had no clue. He didn’t even know where she’d come from, let alone what she had done in the time of her absence.

But it wasn’t of importance. What was, in contrast to that, was that he moved her. They couldn’t stay here. Her long legs were lying on the floor. The tiles were cold, hard. For himself, he wouldn’t have minded. But for her, he wanted a way more comfortable place to rest. It would become a little challenge to reach it, however.
