There doesn't seem to be any internal consensus.” He rubbed the back of his head. “So much that camps are forming?” He watched Chris brows furrow. “Why are you asking?” He placed his mug before them on the low table, rested his elbows to his knees. “I heard rumors that there are… might be disagreements within the BSAA.” Chris looked through the curling smoke around him, as if he debated with himself how far he could go. But then he eased back into backrest behind him. “You are putting it very mildly. Right now, there are very few people I trust. It is why I couldn’t talk to you on the phone. I think I might be monitored.” He nodded. “Well, fuck.” So his sources and Ada, for that matter, had been right. How in hell had she known?

For a moment there was silence. He bowed his head low, thread his fingers deeply into his hair as he tried to think. What had she picked up again that they hadn’t? And from where? Damn this. He now remembered her still being upstairs. He couldn’t hide it any longer. Chris trusted him. He was talking about things he knew could compromise his position in the BSAA. He couldn’t keep sitting here, discussing highly sensitive topics knowing full well they weren’t alone. He needed to tell him. He just… Didn’t know how. Sorry man, somewhat forgot to mention that someone is still waiting for me upstairs. Someone who you think you killed, who you hold responsible for a major bio terror attack. And mostly so because I never filled you in on the truth. He gritted his teeth. Every version he played out in his head sounded insane. Every second that passed made it worse.

Chris broke the silence that had fallen without him noticing. “How are things around DSO?” He shrugged, unenthusiastically. He really didn’t have the mind to speak about DSO right now. “Business as usual, I’d say. Apart from that researcher… There is absolutely nothing worth mentioning.” Chris surveyed him through sharp eyes, for a moment before he spoke again. “Are you thinking about quitting?” His brows shot skywards, leaving his eyes wide in surprise. “What? What makes you think that?” Chris leaned towards him. “What you say and… “ he gestured around “this, Leon. It looks different. You look different. I don’t know how to explain it. To me, honestly, you always seemed pretty screwed up.” He snorted a breath. “Thank you so much.” Chris chuckled dryly. “Yeah, welcome, wasn’t finished though. Fucked up like me, I meant. Swallowed whole by the bullshit we deal with every day. Now though you move here, to a very pretty apartment in Boston, you make coffees that taste better than ones made by a trained barista with decades of professional experience and you sound immensely dispassionate talking about the DSO.…” He broke him off, thought it would be now or never.

“Chris, look, uhm… I have no intention of quitting, seriously, but... You are right. Something has changed. I…” This time Chris interrupted. Staring with attentive perceptive eyes. “You’re kidding me. Or are you telling me you are settling down?” He shrugged, shook his head. “Yeah, I think I have. I, uhm… There is someone.” Chris eyes widened as he glanced around once more. “What the fuck Kennedy? You let me bore you to death with Blue Umbrella and the BSAA all the while not mentioning with a single word that you have met a girl?” He cleared his throat. “Well met isn’t the way I would put it but yeah, in fact, uhm, there is someone. I did not move here alone.” “Why are you so fucking cryptic? Give me all the glory details.” He sighed, wondered about a way to direct this and then got distracted by a chime from his phone. A notification, perhaps a message and he glanced about, spotting his device lying on the coffee table. Right before Chris’ knee. They both automatically looked. The screen had come alive, illuminating a lock screen that showed the picture of a dirty blonde man, kissing the corner of the faintly smiling red lips of a dark-haired woman with dark eyes directed south.

He felt his throat turn sour. His pulse lurched, every muscle in his body tightening as his gaze snapped to Chris. He was staring down blankly. His mouth opened, but he didn’t speak. Then he reached, picked up the phone to hold out its display to him. “What is that, Leon?” He didn’t look, locked gazes with Chris instead. “Chris…” The man’s eyes hardened. “Tell me that’s not who I think it is.” He brought his hand up in a placating gesture. “Chris it isn’t the way it looks” he started but was broken off. “The fuck it isn’t.” Chris shook his head once, hard. “I can explain it.” He was answered by a hollow laugh. "Explain what, exactly? How Ada Wong is your goddamn lock screen? A fucking terrorist?" He closed his eyes briefly. “She isn’t, Chris. It wasn’t her, it was…” “Oh, don't give me that bullshit. Not again. I am not senile. I know Simmons was guilty. But she was the one to fucking orchestra the whole thing. Neo Umbrella was her creation!" He threw the phone to the table. It landed on the glass with a loud clatter.

Chris eyes snapped to the staircase suddenly, then back to him. "Is she here?” He swallowed, hesitated. Long enough to watch Chris’ expression distort into a sneer. And then he rose. He jumped to his feet too, immediately. But Chris didn’t look at him anymore, went around the table to head upstairs. He skipped over his chair and blocked the way.

"...Move the fuck out of my way, Kennedy." He didn’t blink, kept his eyes firmly on Chris’. "I won’t. So calm down." Chris stepped up close, lowered his voice. “I said move aside.” He had to lift his chin now to be able to meet his gaze. “No.” Chris set himself in motion to go around him, he shifted, blocking the path once more. Chris’ shoulder bumped into his.

For one suspended heartbeat neither of them breathed. Then Chris planted a hand against his chest and shoved. Not hard but enough to make his point unmistakably clear. He would not let this go. Acting on not much more than instinct, he immediately pushed back, driving Chris away from the stairs. He stared at him in disbelief. "...You're putting your fucking hands on me?" He noticed that his own eyes had narrowed all by themselves. “I will not let you pass.”

Chris let out a disbelieving huff of air. Then he surged forward. He met him head on. Their shoulders collided with a solid crack. Chris drove into him, hard this time, trying to force him off balance. He dug his heels into the hardwood, absorbing the impact before answering with one of his own after which Chris caught him by the front of his shirt. He seized his forearm. And for a beat they were locked together, muscles straining. And then Chris restraint gave way.

A fist flew in his direction. He dodged, answered with a quick jab to his ribs. But Chris caught his wrist, yanked him forward and drove his shoulder into his chest. The impact rattled through his body. It released aggression. He avoided another punch, years of training had made him very hard to hit cleanly, he had become fast over the years. But Chris was a fucking rock, and when his left hand caught him by the shoulder he blinked. And was punished by a hard fist crashing into his jaw. He groaned, pain exploded through his face, his vision momentarily blurred.

Her voice only reached his ringing ears through a blur and for a second he believed he had just imagined it. Then he lifted his head to look up, still crouching from pain. She stood on the landing. Barefoot, a deep red silken strap dress now covering her form. Only at second glance, he noticed the gun she was holding. He gazed sidewards to realize it was pointed at Chris head.

“You are done, Captain.” Chris mouth opened, but she leveled her gun, shaking her head. Softly, with dark eyes fixated on the man below her she added “Take one more step in his direction. I’ll blow your head off.” He felt his eyes widen as he stared at her. She didn’t waver. Her eyes were hard, resolved. There wasn’t a trace of uncertainty in her stance. No bluff. No mercy. He remained silent, despite knowing he should do something, knowing it should scare him. This situation, her. And in a way it did.

But there was also something else entirely. Something that made his jaw clench and his heart stumble. The realization that she did not seem to aim to threaten Chris. She didn’t demand anything. She hadn’t raised her gun to intimidate him. But to prevent a second punch. She covered his ass and he wasn’t sure if she wouldn’t actually shoot in case Chris decided to make a wrong move right now. And justified or not, rightfully so or wrong. She had his back. Like she’d always had. He momentarily closed his eyes felt his emotions getting the best of him. Then he slowly took a step forward, put himself in her line of fire, shielding Chris from her aim. He locked gazes with her. “Its alright.”

Her eyes flickered to him. But her gun didn’t move. He gave her his most pleading look, watched her blink. And then lower her arm. He felt Chris shove past him though and about to protest, he saw Ada aim back straight and Chris freeze. After a second, she waived with her gun towards the couch. “Do yourself a favor and move the fuck over, Redfield. I mean it.” She pushed her brows slightly upwards, eyes remaining cold. “And maybe then we can talk.” For a moment Chris remained frozen to the spot, breathing hard. Then he collected himself and stalked off. Her gunpoint followed him all the way.

She kept her eyes on Chris’ as she descended the stairs. Her pistol was still in her hand, safety still unlocked. She didn’t look at Leon when she walked past him and came to a halt on the other side of the coffee table, across from the couch where Chris had meanwhile taken a seat. She paused for an instant, surveying his every move, before she let herself down on the chair beside her. On the reverie, she registered that Leon remained standing diagonally behind her, arms crossed before his chest. She swung a leg over the other, looked at the weapon in her hand, waited.

Like she had expected, it only took Redfield a mere second before it broke out of him. “What the fuck are you?” Her gaze snapped back up to his. Explanations had never been her currency. She had never been able to afford the luxury of minding being misunderstood. Somewhere along the way, she had stopped caring about other people's judgments altogether. It rarely amounted to much anyway. Most saw what they expected to see, heard what they wanted to hear, and called it truth. More often than not, anything one added was simply wasted. She thus had no desire to justify herself. Especially not to Chris Redfield of all people.

Her gaze drifted to Leon for the briefest moment. He hadn't moved, arms still crossed, jaw still tight. It was getting to him. He wasn’t indifferent. He had put himself between her and Chris before. And she had seen the look in his eyes, his concern. He cared for this man who had been a companion in battle for years, perhaps become something like a friend to him. She didn’t really know. They didn’t seem to see each other often. And yet there was a quiet alignment between them. Two men, fighting the same fight, each for their individual reasons. But always on the same side. On the side where they thought was justice. And now he was worried about what this would leave behind. And so, she thought, perhaps some truths could be wasted on the people who demanded them.

And wasn’t it interesting anyway? He hadn’t asked who she was. But what. It almost made her smile in recollection. She arched a brow instead. Fury and utter irritation flickered behind Redfield’s eyes. “How are you still sitting here? I shot you, for hells sake. I saw you falling off that damn roof, saw your guts splattering onto the asphalt. There is no way in hell someone could survive this.” She tilted her head. Almost felt compassion. Oh Redfield. Did he really think Leon had brought home a bioweapon? No wonder he was so shaken. “You are wrong” she said calmly. “In every possible way. You didn’t kill me. You did not kill her either. You just think you did.” Chris grimaced in confusion, mouth opening to put something in but she beat him to it. “Don’t worry. I finished the job for you. After you left.”

A deep frown seized his features. But he didn’t speak. Just looked between her and Leon in entire disbelief. She could literally watch the efforts of his synapses to catch up. After an eternity his eyes wandered to eventually lock on Leon. “What the hell is going on here?” he asked, still angry. But she could feel that he was cooling down now. She heard Leon sigh and get in motion. He crossed the room and lowered himself onto the couch on the opposite side of Chris’, leaving as much space between them as possible.

She leaned back slightly. This time, it seemed to take him longer to detangle the mess that was probably unfolding inside his head. But eventually, he looked at her again. Irritation had crept beneath his anger, stripping it of its certainty. “You said I didn’t kill you. And that I also failed killing her. Her. Who is that?” She met his eyes. “Carla Redeems.” He steadily returned her gaze. He had no clue. Actually had not the faintest idea after all these years. Leon had never told him anything. And for a moment it brought her to pause. Then she looked at Chris again. “I have never heard of that name. Who was she? Your twin?” A mirthless laugh escaped her. Perhaps more accurately a guess than was bearable. “Not quite. And yet… She was created to resemble me.” Chris now frowned deeply. He looked as lost as could be. Perhaps no longer even in a place to form a further question from here.

Silence settled over the room. After a moment, Leon leaned forward, his eyes finding hers. They were open, honest. Seeing her, showing her he was there. And holding a subtle question. As if he was wondering whether he was allowed to speak or if he was supposed to remain silent. She assumed that he would respect both.

For a heartbeat, she simply held his gaze. There had been a time she would have looked away. Let the silence stretch until Redfield would explode once more. It would have been easier. But now, she gave the faintest nod. For his sake. And his shoulders eased momentarily. Almost imperceptibly but very obviously to her.

He gave her the softest of all expressions, then turned to Chris. “It started with Simmons. You already know most things about him from my report. What he did, what he worked on. But not with who.” Chris remained silent, listened intently. “You thought it has been her” he nodded in her direction but Chris didn’t look. “But it wasn’t. He worked with Carla Redeems.” Chris brows knitted together, he faintly shook his head. “What the… If it wasn’t her. Then why does she look completely alike?” Leon sighed. “Simmons was… Infatuated with Ada for years. I didn’t know it then…” another glance to her “but she told me in the aftermath of Lanshiang.” Chris now looked at her too. Then back to Leon.

He elaborated, still holding her gaze. “Simmons wanted her under his control. And when he realized he couldn’t have it… Well. He turned to one of his researchers. Carla. She worked on the C-virus for him. He ordered her to use the virus and genetic engineering to recreate Ada.” At that Chris frowned. “Recreated?” he repeated. Leon nodded. “He wanted another Ada Wong. Carla helped him. I don’t know how many failed experiments it took them, how many lives were wasted. But ultimately, Carla succeeded. She unleashed the virus on herself, underwent a transformation to become physically identical with her. I saw the video. I couldn’t tell the difference myself.” His eyes found the floor, as if he felt embarrassed by it. Chris didn’t seem to have caught it.

“Why the fuck would someone have done that?” Leon shrugged vaguely, holding his gaze for a brief moment. The answer seemed painfully obvious to him. “She must have been deeply devoted to him. Perhaps she thought that becoming Ada would finally bring her the validation she sought to receive. Or because she simply loved him.” Chris indistinctly shook his head, deep in thought. “No reason to kill my unit, to infect a capital, to declare war on the whole fucking world for me though” he muttered through gritted teeth. “Simmons never cared for her. Not before and not after, Chris.” The whole man tensed. “Fuck Leon, what are you saying? I lost all my men in Edonia. Piers died in that hive. He was my fucking best friend, my best soldier, my parter. The one who always had my back and who I still dream about every goddamned night.” Leon closed his eyes, ran a hand through his hair. He expression looked pained. Then opened them again. “I am not trying to justify anything Chris. I am just telling you the story. Carla Redeems gave up her face, her identity, everything really. Only to realize that it still hadn’t been enough. It must have infuriated her to the point of insanity. So furious that she wanted so destroy everything.”

Chris gaze snapped to hers. She held it. She hadn’t known that he had lost so much in that battle. She hadn’t been in Edonia. She hadn’t known that he’d had that partner that had also been his friend. It made her feel sorry for him after all. Perhaps Leon had been right. He had deserved to know all along.

“She wanted revenge on Simmons” she said calmly, registered Leons gaze now snapping to her too, entirely surprised. “She infected him. And on me. She left me a trail before everything went down. She wanted me aware. Aware that she would make the world believe I was responsible for Neo Umbrella. That I was a terrorist. It wouldn’t have sufficed to just kill me. She wanted my existence destroyed for all eternity, wanted me haunted beyond death for setting the world on fire. Ironically enough, Simmons refused to let go of me despite it all. Until his very last breath.”

Both men stared at her. Expressions equally empty. She didn’t really know what suddenly moved Leon so much about it, he had known the story. But they both looked as if being forced to reconsider everything they had thought they had known. That the bad guys were the bad guys. That they didn’t deserve compassion. And yet… Sometimes there were things beyond good and bad, things that couldn’t be reconciled with common categories. She of all people knew.

“Why did you not clear this up? I mean… You weren’t responsible. Couldn’t you have proved that? You…” She broke him off. “It wasn’t of importance. The world believed I was dead. And for a person like me… It was not the worst thing that could have happened.” She noticed Leon squeezing his eyes shut. She knew he couldn’t stand it. She knew he hated how he had never been able to protect her, how she had, for the longest time not let him in. It still seemed an unhealed wound.

After an eternity of silence, Chris let his now limp shoulders bump back into the cushions as he never left Leons gaze. “Fuck. Everything makes so much sense now. The way you beat the crap out of me then. The way you didn’t bat an eyelid, not even when I told you that she worked for Neo Umbrella. I never fully understood. Until today. Your fear for her, your resolve to protect her.” Chris shook his head. “It changes everything.” She glanced towards Leon, tried to pick up his gaze but he deliberately avoided her. She had no idea what Chris was talking about. Had he just said that…. Leon had fought with him over Carla? Because he had mistaken her for her? He hadn’t known things then. She had only told him months after everything had been over. So had he protected her despite the non zero chance that she had in fact been responsible for everything? Something in the back of her throat started to burn.

Chris voice in her direction didn’t manage to bring her attention back. What he said just registered on the reverie. “She must have been obsessed with you” he muttered, shaking his head, staring at her before his eyes drifted to the opposite wall. She did no longer know who he was talking about. But she knew now that Leon had taken her side then. Despite the unclarity he had chosen to stand with her no matter what. Perhaps against his own convictions. Certainly against Chris Redfield. Against the whole world. She released a long breath. And finally knew what she had never been certain about. Paradox enough, that it happened as a result of a conversation with Chris fucking Redfield.

He stared at the carpet underneath his feet. Chris statement echoed. She must have been obsessed with you. She had been obsessed with her. Just like Simmons. He had known and yet talking about it again, hearing her speak about it again made him painfully aware of the undeniable facts.

Derek Simmons. Part of one if not the most powerful families within the US. Had reduced himself to something pathetic, perhaps even pitiful. A man who had possessed influence beyond imagination had surrendered every ounce of dignity to an obsession he had mistaken for love. So many lives had been sacrificed, a whole empire had fallen because he hadn’t been able to take no for an answer.

She had walked away from Wesker after Spain, had betrayed him. She had walked away. Wesker had let her walk away. Because would he have wanted her dead, he was convinced he would have had it done. So even monsters had found themselves orbiting her. Men who had believed themselves above everyone else had still looked at Ada and recognized someone they could neither replace nor dismiss.

He thought about the rings he hid in the vast back of his closet, about their relationship, about every moment they’d shared until this very instant where she sat across from Chris Redfield. And it struck him then, more violently than ever before, that this woman was something he truly had no right to ask for. It was presumptuous. Because what did he think he could afford her that they hadn’t offered?

Nothing. He was just a man, no power, averagely intelligent. What did she see in him? How could he have ever assumed that he could even be remotely enough for a woman like her? He had no idea. And yet she was here. With him. Had come to him over and over throughout years. Why the fuck? He thought about Carla Redeems. Why the fuck did people go through insane things? For love. So had she loved him? All the time? Had he gotten her all wrong? Over the years, had he just been too occupied with himself to notice that she had been there all along? How had he been so stupid? How had he never managed to bring himself to find his goddamn guts and try to make her stay? How had he wasted himself on woman after woman that had never felt right? When everything he had ever wanted had been right before his eyes? He had realized she was the one basically even before he had first been allowed to touch her.

But what was done was done, wasn’t it? He couldn’t change it now. He couldn’t make up for it. Perhaps it couldn’t have been changed anyway, perhaps it needed exactly the time it had taken. What he could still do though was to place everything he was into her hands and hope, against every instinct he possessed, that one day it would be enough. That his love would be enough, that a home would be enough which she could leave without explanation and return to without permission. Because he did not have much more to offer. He was a recovering alcoholic who always had one foot in the grave. And no less disgustingly possessive than the other men that had been fucking obsessed with her above it all. He was no fucking exception. But he promised himself right there that he would become better. He would train harder. He would become smarter. Less reckless. Because as much as he had never managed to step out of the shadow of his ego and tell her how dependent he was on her. He would from now on never leave her side again. He would promise it to her.

Through his racing mind, he suddenly realized that Chris had addressed her again. He wasn’t immediately able to catch what he had said, however. He had been too distracted. But he watched her eyes narrow slightly. “We have known each other for a long time.” Chris frowned at that. “I asked from where. Not for how long.” Fuck. His gaze snapped to Ada. Her expression remained perfectly blank, however. “I don’t see why it would be any of your business, Redfield.” Chris leaned further towards her, shoulders broad. “Perhaps it isn’t. But why not simply tell me regardless? Why dodge? Makes it seem like that piece of information is interesting enough to be worth protecting. And I can think of only one reason why this would be the case. So let me put it more frankly. Have you been involved in the Raccoon City incident?” Her eyes hardened. And for a moment she hesitated. Then her eyes flickered to him. He didn’t blink. Left it to her to decide what to answer. She reverted to Chris. “I have been in Raccoon.” “For what purpose? I never saw your name mentioned anywhere. I would have remembered.” They held each other’s gazes. She didn’t blink. “I gave you my answer. And anyway, what is it to you? You shouldn’t be so stuck in the past but rather put your energy into the issues you are presently dealing with. In this regard you might actually still be able to make a change.” His eyes narrowed. “What are you saying?” He pressed, voice rough, bordering on a threat and caused him to almost rise from his seat. But she remained utterly calm. “Easy there, cowboy. I just have a feeling that there might still be some issues to be solved. Even though I hear that your moves are closely watched.” Her eyes were stern. His gaze flickered to Chris, then back to her. He seemed no less clueless than he himself. What the hell was she talking about?

Suspicion grew behind Chris eyes, before he looked at him, intently, searching his eyes. He allowed it, just looked back with all the sincerity a man could muster. After a second Chris sighed, seemingly just deciding to throw his reservations in the wind. "...I've had that feeling for a while." The words were reluctant. "Every operation we shut down..." He frowned. "Somebody else steps in before the dust even settles. I wanted to look into things. But they just didn’t listen. Like I was hallucinating. There was suddenly no money, doubts were spread, resources were cut off.” That seemed to have raised her attention, her eyes flashed with something immediately. “Looking into things like what?” Chris locked gazes with her and for a moment they stared at each other. Like charged wild animals. He just watched, holding his breath.

“That information is classified” Chris said eventually and her look hardened. “Not so much as you think.” His eyes narrowed. “What?” “I answered many of your questions. Now I am asking you one of my own. The investigation you mentioned, what was it about? Missing persons in Louisiana and a vessel?” His eyes went wide. “What the fuck?” he muttered. She folded her arms before her chest. “Not a bad guess, huh?” Chris eyes wandered unfocused before they locked back on hers. He showed her his cigarette package, nodded towards the terrace. “On a word?” He opened his mouth to protest but she suddenly looked at him, nodding faintly. He sighed in defeat. Fuck he didn’t like this. Didn’t like this at all. But he didn’t seem to have much of a choice as he watched them get up and head outside only seconds later.

The fresh morning air had considerably warmed up. The days were getting milder in general. It felt nice. She wasn’t always freezing anymore.

He shut the sliding door behind them, the muffled sound of Leon moving about inside fading almost entirely. For a moment neither of them spoke. He pulled a cigarette from the pack without offering her one too, lit it with practiced ease and drew deeply before regarding her over the ember.

"What do you know about Louisiana?" She rested both hands on the railing. The city stretched out beneath them, sunlight sparkled over the facades and reflected in windows like little stars. "That the lead is real." He remained expressionless. “How do you know that? My investigation led nowhere.” She nodded slowly. “Yeah. It seems like whoever is against you in this thoroughly made sure you were distracted enough to cease your efforts once you didn’t get immediate results.” "Distracted?" She smiled a mirthless smile. “Redfield you are stuck in the past. You have just impressively proved that point.” It earned her a glare that she ignored as she went on. “You see an Umbrella logo you go after it like a hound dog after its prey.” “What the fuck are you implying?” She turned her head just enough to look at him. “Blue Umbrella might not be as bad as you think it is. But it has a lot to offer regardless. Familiar personnel. Some dubious black market activities. A disappearing scientist. Spread over organizations to make it believable. Not bad at all. They knew you would bite. And overlook what’s really important.”

He took another drag. “And that is?” His voice was still sharp. edged with aggression. But he seemingly was making an effort to keep it contained. She smiled. “To follow that trail." “Do you know where it leads?” he challenged. She shook her head, just faintly. Then she surveyed his eyes. “That information is classified” she recited him with a faint smirk. Then continued, this time more seriously. “Everything I would tell you from here would be said too much. You need a clean trail. What you do must not lead back to me.” He kept staring her down. It didn’t bother her. Perhaps she was getting comfortable around him. Maybe it had something to do with the increased distance between him and Leon now. "Find the missing people, Redfield. And don’t let yourself be misled by medical reports dating back longer than three years. Everything in this regard is fake.” She had thought and rethought about it, still didn’t know for sure who had made them up. All she knew was that they were a purposedly set up wrong trail. The younger medical reports, however, seemed to have been real. She had kept searching for the patients over the last weeks. Hadn’t managed to track down even a single one. Neither did it seem like either of them had families. Or jobs. Or social security numbers. It was as if they’d never existed. Nobody had filed them missing. All their traces had been comprehensively erased. It could only mean an involvement of either the Government which she doubted or The Connections themselves. She assumed that the victims had been recaptured by the Bakers or the subject following their respective medical treatments. She couldn’t tell for sure, however.

Chris Redfield beside her kept smoking with furrowed brows, staring in silence. "Why are you telling me this?” he asked after a long moment. Her eyes drifted toward the horizon. “Because somebody has to take care of it and because I know you have already been on the trail.” He studied her, like he was trying to read what was going on behind her eyes. Generations before him had tried. She kept her countenance blank. Then the cigarette lowered slightly between his fingers.

"How do you even know that?" She remained perfectly still as he elaborated, probably not even actually meaning her but just thinking aloud. "I never told Leon. I never filed any report. What I had on the vessel wasn't shared with anyone outside my personal task force." His voice grew quieter. "It was still preliminary. Everything I had..." he blinked, swallowed "...was on my personal computer."

She let the silence stretch. Didn’t react at all. “You hacked me" he muttered, and for a second she couldn’t tell if he sounded offended or impressed. Then his eyes widened into a kind of horror she had rarely ever witnessed. Probably not even on civilians in Raccoon City. “What did you see?” She held his gaze. Just shrugged. “I needed to know if you would become a problem. I was told it could be. Which you should take as a warning for yourself. Someone is watching you or the BSAA from the shadows.” He gritted his teeth, repeated himself. “What. Did. You. See?” She huffed an annoyed breath, didn’t see any reason for him to piss his pants now. “Don’t worry. I kept it strictly business” she answered. Couldn’t help, however, applying a tone that she assumed would make him full well aware of the fact that she knew that not all of his files had qualified into that category. She distinctly suppressed as smile as she could almost watch his mind racing, probably assessing the damage, going over every photo he had on his laptop which captured Jill. And as the heavy silence stretched on, a sly smirk escaped her despite her best efforts. He took note of it in silent frustration, kept staring at her. “Shoot me” he muttered, eyes unfocussed, faded off into the reverie. She eased her expression back into emotionlessness. “I was about to before. Give me a reason and I might be willing to reconsider.” He drew in a sudden, ragged breath, worried eyes now uncharacteristically pleading and she wondered why he, now of all moments, opted to not try and threaten her instead. “Look, I don’t know what you think you saw but…” She broke him off. Somehow, to her surprise, she found it disarming. Couldn’t bring herself to keep playing that game, found his apparent suffering hard to endure. Perhaps because he reminded her too much of Leon right now. “Relax Captain. I don’t work and tell.”

She waited. For another accusation. Another demand. Another attempt to interrogate her. None came. Instead he remained where he stood, shoulders drawn taut beneath his leather jacket, gaze fixed somewhere beyond the terrace. He lit another cigarette, spoke with it dangling from his mouth.
