He bit the insides of his cheeks to finally stop himself from smiling. “Come in” Christina said and stepped aside to let them enter. “Declan, my neighbors are here” she called as they went inside.

The place was a lot smaller than theirs but cozy. The interior was done in shades of cream, ivory and pale wood. The sort of setup magazines would probably call timeless. Candles had been lit everywhere. Several potted plants softened the room. He eyed them with mild suspicion before silently wishing them luck. In his experience, plants had an uncanny talent for dying the moment one accepted responsibility for them.

A male voice came from somewhere deeper inside the apartment. “Coming.” And an instant later a man stepped out of the kitchen, carrying some plates. He was tall, around their age, with perhaps slightly too untrimmed reddish blonde hair. He had an open face, an easy smile and an accent that immediately gave away his origin. He set the plates down on the table and approached them, extending Ada his hand. She accepted. “So you’re the woman who fixed plumbing with less hesitation than most people pour a drink.” She tilted her head in acknowledgement. “I thought about looking horrified to satisfy the cliches, but it felt dishonest.” Declan barked out a laugh loud enough to startle Christina and he couldn’t help but chuckle too. “Oh Jesus, she’s funny too. You could have at least warned me” he said without taking his eyes off her for even a beat. When he finally managed to tear his gaze away from her and looked to him, he took a step in his direction to give him a rather rough shoulder clap. “You must be Leon. I saw your motorbikes downstairs man. Not a bad collection.” “Thanks.” Christina jumped back in.

“Why don’t we have a drink? What can I get you? We have wine or would you like to try my homemade lemonade? All organic ingredients, of course.” Ada answered without a seconds hesitation. “That sounds perfect.” Christina smiled and looked at him. He nodded quickly. “Same for me.” “Does she not let you drink, or are you one of those guys who avoids anything that might hinder muscle growth?” Declan asked a little teasingly. He forced a smile. “Uhm, no, I am just actually a little thirsty, that’s all.” It sounded simple. Almost casual. But inside, it entirely wasn’t casual. Every invitation, every gathering seemed to resolve into the same shape. Nice food, conversations. And drinks. The world had a script for social ease, and alcohol was always written into it. He had never realized how deeply rooted this was in society until he had gone sober. “Which is not a bad thing” Christina put in in Declan’s direction. “Perhaps you should follow his example.” Declan rolled his eyes but composed himself quickly. “Anyway, come on guys. Sit down. Dinner is almost ready.”

The evening settled surprisingly well after that. Their hosts had served roasted potatoes, vegetables and sliced chicken. The moment nobody was paying attention, he quietly picked the meat from her plate with his fork. She looked at him briefly, a warm expression easing over her features as she continued listening to Declan. The man was easy to talk to. The kind of person who filled silence naturally instead of anxiously. Christina relaxed visibly around him too, constantly touching his arm when she passed by or leaning against his shoulder without thinking. He noticed Ada watching it, almost looking curious. Like she found uncomplicated displays of affection somewhat fascinating. Like she was right now evaluating how a normal relationship was supposed to look like. As if she didn’t know. It was strange. But then again, he wondered if she wasn’t simply just like this, quietly surveying, observing. He couldn’t really tell. Being invited together with her to dinners was a novelty. Being around others as a couple in general was a novelty. It was a new side of her to explore. And he was eager for it, caught his eyes flickering back to her all the way as they talked about Boston winters, about bad coffee, about restaurants around town and motorbikes.

She sat beside him in an effortlessly sophisticated posture. Declan talked about Dublin for a while, about rain and loud relatives and missing proper tea in America. Christina rolled her eyes fondly, then looked at them. “He literally brought his own tea in his suitcase.” He smiled in response, Declan let out an annoyed huff. “Correct. Because you don’t even do different sorts here. All you have is black and then add some flavor to tell yourself you got variety. It’s a crime.” He laughed into his lemonade, unable to stop himself. “You pretty much sound like Ada when I attempted to bring my capsule coffee machine to this place.” He heard her chuckle softly beside him. “Even though now you have to admit that I was right to refuse calling capsule machine drinks coffee, don’t you?” He opened his mouth to retort something when Declan waived his hand dismissively. “Of course he has. And Chrissy as well. She drinks my tea all the time.” He watched them exchange a soft smile, mirrored it before Christina looked back at him. “So when you say you weren’t allowed to bring your coffee machine… I am curious. Are you both originally from Boston?” He shook his head quickly. “No, no. I, uhm I mean we, we moved here from Washington D.C.” Christina nodded. “Ah, interesting. I mean, I kind of noticed that you two aren’t always around. Not that I pried, or anything… Just…” She broke off, scratched the back of her head in mild embarrassment. He just gave her a kind reassuring expression. “Yeah, its right. I still work in DC.” From the corner of his eyes he saw Ada’s gaze snap to him. He, too, had no clue why he had volunteered that information. It had just slipped as a result of the easy atmosphere. And he decided so what. There was nothing wrong with telling your neighbors something about you, was there?

“Federal?” Declan suddenly asked. For a moment he held his gaze, before he answered. “Yeah.” “Thought so ” Declan said. “You have that exhausted vibe.” He blinked. Ouch. “Wow” he muttered. “That hurts.” There was a brief chuckle in the air and he glanced at Ada who looked a bit too entertained for his taste. “And you?” Christina asked, now in Ada’s direction and he froze. Every fiber of his body was suddenly alert, ready to defend. But when he looked, he found that she had remained entirely relaxed. “I am an investigator.” Brows raised across the table. “Oh, interesting. For a federal agency?” “No. Private.” “A private investigator?” He regarded Ada as she spoke. She held steady eye contact with her audience, moved her lips purposedly. Spoke with mimic, making them hang on her lips. He assumed from this expression they would buy whatever she sold them. It was kind of fun to watch. Utterly scary at the same time. She deliberately took a sip from her drink. “Yes. I look into… corporate matters.” Their faces fell as if they were now disappointed that she hadn’t revealed to them that she spied on unfaithful husbands. “Right” Christina concluded. “And what brought you here, to Boston if not work?” When he realized Ada didn’t answer he shrugged. “Change of scenery, mostly. We needed something new.” Which wasn’t even much of a lie. “So you grew tired of DC? Were you born there?”

He shook his head, kept answering automatically. “No, I was born in Warren, Pennsylvania. I grew up there.” Declan raised his brows in interest. “Never heard of” he admitted. He smiled. “Yeah, no surprise. It’s small, quiet. Lots of trees. Everybody knew each other.” “Sounds like every city in Ireland if you ask me” Declan put in and chuckled. He smiled faintly too. Then Declan turned to Ada, expression curious. “And you? Small town girl, too?” He glanced at her. She didn’t flinch, didn’t blink, simply retained her posture, one leg elegantly placed behind the other, hands interlaced in her lap. For a second he wondered what she would make up, what story she was just now inventing behind her dark calculating eyes. Whatever it was, he was immensely intrigued to hear.

“Not quite” she said and then added in an unusually soft tone “I was born in Macau”. And somehow, the room went quiet after that. His gaze was locked on her. So was Declan’s. Christina snapped out of it first. “Really? Wow, that’s amazing. I have been there once. I remember all the tiled streets and the Portuguese buildings. It is so beautiful.” He watched a smile spread on Ada’s lips but her eyes… They were not smiling. Almost… Cold. She blinked it away in a beat, let only the cheerful expression remain. “Yes. The city is beautiful” she allowed and from the looks of Declan and Christina they took it as affirmation. But he kept his eyes focused on her. And he realized it hadn’t been. On the contrary, she had just told them that she hadn’t liked the city at all, just nobody had noticed. He kept staring as his eyes widened, as his lips parted, as his breath hitched when it slowly sank in. It had been the truth. She was from Macau. His jaw dropped open despite his best efforts and suddenly she looked at him. He felt his brows furrow, realized he had stopped breathing. She just kept holding his gaze.

Then she gave him the faintest of all smiles, allowing him to read her for once before she averted her eyes, fingertips lightly touching the stem of her water glass. He forgot the room around them existed. After all these years, he finally knew where she had been born. She had told them. Just like fucking that. He swallowed. Tried to pick back up eye contact. But she didn’t look anymore and it was Declan who cut into his suddenly foggy state of mind. “And what brought you to the US?” She gave a small shrug, as if she was really thinking. When she looked up, however, her eyes were firm again, all softness vanished. She crossed her arms before her chest and leaned back into her chair. “I needed a change of scenery” she repeated with a playful smirk that, in combination with her posture made perfectly clear that their conversation about Macau was over. And still all eyes remained on her. She had this aura again, the million mysteries sparkling underneath her marvelous surface drawing them in. It was irresistible.

Christina stepped in once more. “The only constant in life is change” she said philosophically, most likely to lighten the now miscellaneous mood with some trite remark and Declan laughed. But Ada just chuckled faintly, answered with a depth that knocked the breath out of his lungs all over. "Change is a curious thing. And a chance, sometimes a challenge. I always welcomed it. And yet… every now and then, I find a certain comfort in discovering that some things remain the same.”

Her eyes flicked toward him, only for one brief second. But for this fleeting instant, she let him see. Let him see that she knew the weight of what she had just disclosed to him. On the reverie, he heard Declan remark something about how pleasant the evening was with them, how he enjoyed the exchange. He couldn’t listen. All he could do was to keep staring at her. To a point where he believed they might notice. But he didn’t care. He didn’t care about them. He cared about her. So much that it itched to sit here normally. He wanted to pull her close, wanted to feel her, wanted to somehow show her how he felt right now, underneath the restraint he tried to maintain.

And then she turned her head to him, fully meeting his eyes at last. He drowned in the depth of hers, watched a small smile dance over her lips, a tilt of her head, an arched brow. Was she wondering something? The smile widened before she reverted her attention back to the table. “Not for me, please.” He now looked, too, was met by Declan’s questioning frown. He pushed his brows skywards, aiming for an apologetic expression. “Sorry. I… uhm… was drifting.” Declan smiled knowingly. “Yeah, I realized man. I was just asking if we should move to some proper drinks?” He opened his mouth to say something, something like he needed to get up early, something like he just didn’t feel like it, perhaps teasing something about ruining his training routine after all. But he closed his mouth again, drew in a deep breath. From the corner of his eye he could see that she, too, was looking at him now. Always ready to protect him.

But he realized he didn’t need that protection anymore. She wanted him around. She really wanted him around. She had proved it over and over. She had said she loved him. She had answered Chris’ questions. And above all, she had surrendered something about herself just now that he would never have expected to ever learn. And so, for the very first time, he thought he felt confident enough to not hide. To not retreat behind a lie. Just be himself. Because perhaps it was enough. So he shook his head lightly. “I’m okay.” Declan paused politely. “Sure?” He felt her eyes on him, met them, gave the smallest tilt of his head. “I’m sober” he admitted. “A little over a year now.” Silence fell for an instant and Christina looked mortified. “Oh my God, I’m sorry…” But he cut her off immediately. “Not at all. It’s okay. You couldn’t know.”

Declan nodded once, respectful and easy about it. “Good thing, Leon” he commented. And that was it. No interrogation followed and no weirdness. His eyes drifted back to her. She didn’t look startled, or pitiful. Somehow unreadable as always. But soft. So unbearably soft. He had never talked about it. Obviously not with anyone else, but also not with her. Or perhaps especially not with her. He had wanted to stuff it into the back corner of his memories, had wanted to forget. Had prayed for her to forget, too. The things she had endured with him. The way he had humiliated himself. It still stung. It was still ugly, small, weak. Disgusting. But she had just told him how important he was for her. Macau. The truth hidden inside her words about change. And he realized that it somehow made him feel less weak. But confident enough to endure his own defaults.

Her foot brushed his calf lightly beneath the table. His breath caught stupidly hard at that tiny contact. Christina began talking again, about a gallery that had recently opened downtown and Ada seemed to have turned her attention back to her. But he didn’t listen anymore, not really. He just sat, noticing the plates emptying together with their glasses while stuck on an image of younger Ada walking Macau streets with tiny feet. With fears? With sorrows? Beside someone? With friends? Or alone? A whole life he had never seen. But that he knew had hurt her in one way or the other. Which he desperately wanted to make up to her.

Later, after they had left, long past one in the morning, they ended up back on the terrace. Mostly because she had claimed she needed some fresh air and at the same time declined his suggestion to take a walk outside. Perhaps, he thought, as they lay on the large outdoor daybed in the vast corner of the space, against piled cushions and underneath thick wool blankets, because she had wanted to do exactly this.

Boston glittered below them in fractured gold and white, the harbor lights trembled softly in the distance beneath a crystal-clear sky. The air had cooled enough to give off a faint vibe of lingering winter but her warm body was close with his arm wrapped around her middle. She had thrown one of his hoodies over her evening wear that swallowed her hands completely. Some rifle spare parts lay unused and forgotten across the table between the lounge chairs, abandoned after the interruption on the previous day. The music she had put on drifted faintly from the outdoor speakers. Old jazz. Low enough to blend into the city noise.

He breathed in her scent as he brushed his forehead against her neckline, planted soft kisses to her nape, her jaw, the sensitive skin just behind her ears. His palm wandered to her front, inside the hoody, stroking the skin of her belly. He pressed himself a little closer, his legs loosely resting to her sides, felt her ease into him. She was relaxed tonight. Had been all day, perhaps even entirely lately. She felt so close to him, not only physically, right now. But emotionally.

She twisted in his embrace and he loosened his arm to allow for her to halfway turn and face him. He adjusted to her, pulled one knee up to give her space as she placed her legs over his other, supported her shoulders with his arm. She watched him as he shoved a strand back behind her ear that had loosened. Her eyes were endlessly dark, not reflecting any light. Wide awake. How often had he seen these eyes and wondered what was passing behind them. He still had no clue, had no idea what she was thinking right now. If she was thinking about the evening, if she was thinking about him, if she was thinking about something else entirely.

He thought about Macau, he had never been there. He wondered what it was like, the city. Where she had lived, how she had looked as a child. What she had dreamed of. How a Chinese girl ended up in the USA, where an innocent child had learned the skills to conquer an outbreak in a fallen city.

He observed her pressing her tongue over her lower lip, noticed a faint tilt of her head. She was looking at him, he realized, immediately lowering his gaze to avoid her. He didn’t want to be greedy tonight. She had said a lot. He didn’t want her to feel like whenever she gave him an inch he would take a mile, too scared that it would keep her from opening up entirely in future. Because he wanted her to. Not only because he wanted to know but also because he wanted to feel safe for her. Safe to be confided in, patient enough to not ask for more than she was willing to give.

But the things one intended and the way they worked out were sometimes not entirely congruent. And a cautious, very brief glance at her that happened despite his best efforts revealed that she had seen right through him already. Damn, from her he was never able to hide and a second later he felt soft fingers catching his chin. He allowed her to lift his gaze back to hers. “Something on your mind, Handsome?” she asked in a voice so gentle that it sent tickers down his spine. He considered denial, considered just kissing the moment away. A sigh escaped him before he realized it.

She closed her eyes, nuzzled into his chest, placing her cheek to his collarbone. He wrapped both arms around her and for a long moment they just stayed like this. Then she whispered, almost inaudible, almost lost to the wind. “Ask.” His heart skipped a beat. She must have felt it, because she began stroking his chest, underneath his now only lazily buttoned up shirt. He closed his eyes, too.

“What is your favorite memory of Macau?” Her caresses paused. But eventually her hand resumed its slow path along his skin. It took another while for her to speak again, however. Perhaps because she had truly been thinking about his question. Perhaps because she had originally not intended to answer at all. “The harbor, I think” she admitted after a long silence. He furrowed his brows. “The harbor?” “Yes. I sometimes watched the ferries at night. I was drawn to their lights. I used to think that they could take me everywhere I wanted to go.” He looked down at her, could hardly see much apart from the smooth line of her forehead, the immaculate tip of her nose. “I would sit there and make up stories about the people on them. Where they lived. Who they loved. What their homes looked like.”

“Hmm” he hummed as he tried to picture it. A little girl sitting by the harbor, a little girl with her infinitely deep eyes. A little girl daydreaming. A little, completely innocent version of her, before lies and guns and ambitious men. A girl with only one name. He swallowed hard. “And what did they look like?” he asked. “Who?” “The homes” he clarified softly, felt her shrug. “Warm. Filled with people waiting who loved them. With plenty of candy in the cupboards…” he heard a faint smile resonating in her voice before it faded again “…with lights that were always kept on.” He deeply inhaled, nodded. Had she longed for a home like this then? When he had walked back to the orphanage from school on winter afternoons and looked inside the illuminated, lavishly decorated windows that had framed his way. Had there been a little Chinese girl sitting by the harbors at the same time that had, just like him, looked from the outside and wished herself to be let in?

His voice faltered a little as he spoke again. “How old were you when you left?” She sighed heavily and he felt himself tense. Perhaps he was pushing her too far. He had avoided asking her about her family, about her childhood home, too afraid of what this question would do to her. But what if this was way too much already? Why did he keep asking anyway when he had intended to keep his mouth shut for once?

“I was thirteen.” His breath got stuck in his throat, his eyes snapped open. What? Thirteen? He stared into the darkness beyond the terrace, thought about himself when he had been that age. Still collecting trading cards. Sneaking extra desserts from the kitchen and staying up way too late with the other boys whenever the caretakers hadn’t looked too closely. A child. A skinny insolent child.

It blurted out of him before he was able to properly think through it. “You were a child with thirteen, Ada. How…. Did you survive? I mean, how did you even manage to get out? Where did you go?” He shook his head in disbelief, couldn’t picture it. There was a long silence accompanied by even breathing. Then she shifted a little until she looked up at him. He could fully see her face now. Cheeks slightly flushed from the warmth of his chest. For a moment she regarded him. There was concern in her eyes, reservation, but he didn’t think it was for herself. It rather seemed like she was wondering whether he was suited to hear what she was going to say. He held her gaze, hoping it would tell her he would endure everything for her. That he could take even the hardest truths in case she felt like sharing them. He had promised her.

“I stowed away on one of those ferries after all. To Hong Kong. Could have been anywhere though. I was on my own.” He grimaced slightly. “You ran off just by yourself? With nothing? How did you even get by? You were a minor.” She shrugged, arched a brow, something bitter suddenly ghosting her expression, something almost spiteful. “When you need to survive you learn very quickly what you can give to people in exchange for money. Without requiring papers.” He couldn’t look at her anymore. It hurt. So bad, he couldn’t think straight. It burned and burned and burned. He felt his stomach turn, felt his throat turn sour. Was entirely unable to comprehend. She had been right, he wasn’t able to take it. He felt like breaking. She had just been a child.

Suddenly, he felt soft fingers cup his face, thumbs brushing his cheekbones, demanding his eyes to open. He did. He owed it to her, at least to not hide. Tears he had tried so hard to suppress fell. She just kept looking at him. “I survived” she said, voice even, a sharp contrast to his dissolution. He looked at her face through his blurred vision, desperately trying to reconcile what she had just told him. And he didn’t know what was worse. This or the question of how terrible her home must have been when it had appeared to her as the better option. It tore him apart as he kept looking at her. At the woman holding him in the palms of her hands.

At the woman who rarely spoke about herself. Who had always been elusive, vague, existing in fragments, and never more. All those years, when he had thought she had been playing with him or deemed him not important enough or otherwise not suitable to know. When perhaps she had just been protecting herself. And him, too. From these truths. From the knowledge that the little girl who watched ferries at night and dreamed of warm homes had also known things no one, whether child or adult, should ever have to.

She kissed away another tear. And it made it so much worse because he should have been comforting her and not the other way around. Damn himself. But she didn’t even look like she needed comfort. There was nothing in her eyes. Absolute blankness. Much like always. Just composure. As if she had made peace with this part of her past a long time ago. He didn’t think he ever would, assumed it alone could easily make him hate the world forever.

The many things he now knew about her. Retrospectively it made all these years where he had craved her, cursed her for running away, hated her for leaving him on the outside seem so small. So damn insignificant. He had been so naïve thinking that with a person like her it could work just like with any other one. When it was a wonder already that she could care for someone, let alone love. An ordinary guy like him. When she could have had others. Perhaps every other.

Slowly, carefully, he covered one of her hands with his. Held it closer against his face. And then leaned in to kiss her. Softly. Innocently, on her lips. He didn’t deepen it. Just pulled back to look at her. At the woman he had spent years chasing and losing and finding again. A part of him he had never been able to let go. He wanted to give her everything she had ever dreamed about. Be every version of himself she wanted to have. Just like he wanted every version of her there was. He drew in a deep breath and then untangled himself from the blankets.

It earned him a surprised expression. “Where are you going?” He stood, ran a hand through his hair to smooth it down. “I’ll be right back” he said, voice coarse. She arched her brow. “…Should I be concerned?” “Probably” he answered honestly. He heard her laugh softly behind him as his heartrate accelerated significantly.

He headed upstairs, pulled open the drawer where he kept his belts and watches. He took out the little box he had picked at the jeweler weeks ago. He stared at it for a second. This was insane. Absolutely insane. He had aimed for restraint and now he would end up pushing her over every possible edge instead. Again. Fuck. He could still pretend he had just needed to use the bathroom. But no. Hell no. He wanted her. And he wanted her aware of it. Wanted to make a statement that the past was in the past. And that she belonged to someone now. That she now had the home she had always imagined. Someone waiting for her who loved her. And who would leave the light on for her forever.

He drew in a deep breath. Okay. He would do it. Even though this hadn’t been the plan. Even though perhaps a lighter evening, lighter topics would have been preferable. Perhaps a home cooked dinner. Perhaps candles. Nothing happening in the middle of the night after learning ugly truths. Maybe even actual emotional preparation. But the hell with it. He was an emotional wreck most of the time anyway. And he decided to start walking before he would lose his courage.

When he stepped back onto the terrace, she sat on the daybed, blankets draped over her shoulders. It made him smile despite his nerves. She watched him as he moved to sit back down beside her. He opened one arm toward her. She answered his invitation without hesitation, settling between his legs to face him, her knees bracketing his hips. The blanket had slipped askew. He gathered it, placed it over her shoulders once more and carefully tucked it around her before resting one hand on her thigh. His heart hammered so hard it felt humiliating and louder than the music. His fingers tightened around the box.

She was so close. He could feel her breath on his lips, spoke very quietly. “I wasn’t planning to do this tonight.” It earned him a deeply questioning gaze bordering on a frown. He ignored it, just slowly opened his palm between them to reveal its content. He felt her gaze flicker between his hands and his face. Other than that, not much changed. Perhaps apart from the frown becoming slightly more distinct. He snapped the cap open and watched her eyes widen before she stopped moving entirely. He could almost witness the realization hitting her as she stared at the rings. It made him wonder if he had ever seen her this clueless before. He assumed he would, for once, have to clarify. But he suddenly found it incredibly hard to even start. He drew in a deep breath then cleared his throat.

“This was supposed to be less chaotic” he apologized in advance “but I did a lot of thinking. About you, about us. About the way you make me feel. In fact, since our very first encounter not a single day has passed where you haven’t been on my mind.” His pulse thrummed against his ribs. „And even though I admit that these thoughts have not always only been bright, deep down inside, all along the way, I think I knew that there could never truly be anyone else.” He huffed, thought about the years of her cashing into and out of his life. “On Sherry’s birthday I was about to tell Claire you were my girlfriend when I realized… I don’t know. It just didn’t really fit. It doesn’t fit something that has become everything you never dared to even dream about and more.”

Her eyes lifted to his. And what he saw was not composed. It was raw. It was attentive. And utterly terrified. “I love you so damn much” he whispered. “All of you. Every single facet. I love you more and more the more you show me of who you are.”

She kept staring at him, unmoving. “I want to be yours, Ada. Unmistakably yours, no turning back. For everyone to see. I know that there isn't a single fucking thing I can do to change the past. And believe me I would give anything for it. What I can do, however, is influence tomorrow. As many days as life still gives us. I want to be the place you come back to. The light that's still on.” He lowered his gaze, bit his lower lip, knew he really should stop talking at this point. But it wouldn’t have been honest. And this was not the place to hold back anymore. This was the time to be brave like he had been when he had first told her he loved her.

He met back her eyes, felt heat creep up his neck and spread into his cheeks like a chain reaction. “I know I am not perfect. There are very ugly parts of me. The ones I fought hard with to convince them that I could live with what we already have. To convince myself I wasn’t possessive. Because I truly want to be different, better than… they have been. Hell, I would want to be a damn saint for you, Ada. But I ain’t. And it would be blasphemy if I claimed I didn’t want you to be mine every fucking bit as much as I want to be yours.”

Her eyes shut instantly. Like the words physically hurt. He thought he’d lose his nerve, forced himself to go on regardless. “"I know..." his voice faltered but he did not look away "…I know that's a lot. Or way too much. But I can’t pretend that it isn’t what I want.”

Her breathing had become so shallow he could barely see her chest rise beneath the blankets. He sighed heavily. Searched her eyes to find only disorientation. As if she’d lost the ground under her feet. Slowly, he reached, very carefully enveloped her fingers in his hand. They were cold. He thumbed over her knuckles. She let it happen.

“I don’t expect you to go to a courthouse with me” he added carefully. “Honestly, I don’t expect anything from you. You don’t have to give me a huge promise. And you certainly don’t owe me a yes. But I want you to know that I’d choose you, in every universe. I’d choose my path over and over again because it crossed yours. And wherever you will be, whatever you are going through, I want this to remind you that I will be here waiting for you to come home. And that I won’t stop loving you. That you won't lose me because…. Well. You won’t lose me. Ever. Period”

His hand trembled slightly as he took one of the rings. The smaller one. He turned her palm, placed the metal in it and closed her fingers around it. “You decide if you want to wear it. You decide which meaning you want to give it. And also if you want to give it one at all. I promise to love you until my last breath. Whenever that will be. Tomorrow, in a month or in forty years.”

When she looked back at him, with shimmering moist eyes, a single tear escaped her ever enigmatic gaze. And that hit harder than anything else tonight because she almost never let herself be seen like this. Not even by him. Big girls didn’t cry. There had only been one singular exception. The moment when she had told him she loved him.

After an eternity, she glanced back at the box that now only held one ring anymore. And he noticed with a painful tear in his chest that she had kept holding onto the second. She covered her eyes briefly with her other hand like she physically could not cope with him anymore. He waited. Didn’t push. Didn’t breathe much either. He felt his pulse everywhere. In his throat. His hands. In every fiber of his heart. She sat impossibly still against him, before she lowered her hand again, leaving herself looking entirely overwhelmed.

She stared at the single ring for another long moment and then brushed her fingertips lightly over it. Very careful. Then she whispered, almost to herself “I never even thought about this.” He swallowed. He had expected as much. “Yeah, I know.” “No” she breathed, now looking at him. “I mean… ever.” He understood. For most his life he had felt the same. Bonds belonged to normal people. To futures. To permanence. Not to people like them.

For another instant she remained entirely motionless, then she picked the ring up to turn it between her delicate fingers. He watched her expression change again as she looked at it. Not fear perhaps now. Definitely reservation, however. Maybe the slightest flash of something in her eyes as she saw the butterfly. “You’re terrifying sometimes, Leon Kennedy.” His body nearly gave out. “Yeah?” ”Yes.” He had to close his eyes for a second. He couldn’t stand the way she looked right now. When he opened them again, she was regarding his hands. And then, moving far steadier than her expression had led him to expect, she took his left hand and carefully slid the ring onto his finger. He held perfectly still, stared at it, his brain stopping to function entirely. Her fingers lingered. Warm, untrembling. His throat tightened so violently he couldn’t say anything. And then a faint, fragile smile appeared on her mouth. “Suits you” she murmured softly. He let out one disbelieving choked huff. “You think?” Her gaze remained firmly stuck on the ring now resting against his skin, material solid enough to make him feel the weight. His chest hurt so much he thought he would cry. She stared down in complete silence. The platinum caught the terrace light softly. Simple. Elegant. Permanent. He watched emotions move through her one after another too quickly to fully name. Some fear. Wonder. Vulnerability. Then finally she lifted her eyes back to him.

And he prayed that, when his time came, the last thing he would ever carry with him from this world would be exactly the look she was giving him now. “You really mean that, Leon?” He felt something inside him go completely unguarded. “Ada” he murmured gently. “I've never been more serious about anything.” A palm was placed to his cheek, fingers slid into his hair to curl in his stands. He closed his eyes, felt her warm breath graze his skin. And an instant later, impossibly gently, barely more than a whisper, she let their lips touch.

Chapter End Notes

What can I say. The title tells the story enough. I can only hope that the take on this is believable and in character. I found it challenging, to say the least. I would love to hear your feedback and thoughts. I love discussing these things. Take care <3

Chapter 11: Unmasked

She couldn’t sleep. She had closed her eyes what felt like hours ago, but her racing mind just didn’t let her shut down. As far as she could tell it wasn’t even something particular that bothered her. She couldn’t put her finger on it. She was just… Restless. She had snuggled into his embrace for warmth and then found it too hot. And now, facing the opposite wall and with a good deal of space between them she realized she was freezing. She pulled the blanket tighter, curled her legs. It didn’t help.

She did not usually struggle with sleep. It just came. But now… She was wide awake. Her heartbeat was too fast. Her senses were too alert. Damn this. She made another attempt to control her breathing, to slow it down, to think about nothing but the heaving of her lungs. As she deeply inhaled, she felt her eyelids heavy. She exhaled a long breath. Perhaps it would work. She yawned.

Noticed a shift of the mattrass, a tremor. A shiver. She blinked her eyes back open in annoyance, looked over her shoulder. The light was barely enough to outline his profile. He had turned on his back, was trembling, breathing far too quickly. His brow was tightly drawn, his jaw clenched. One hand had twisted itself into the bed sheet. His knuckles stood out white. His head gave the slightest shake, refusal or denial, she couldn’t tell. But his hair was soaked in sweat, his skin glistened.

She watched him for a moment. It didn’t happen often. But there were these nights. These nights during which he was haunted. Haunted by nightmares he never remembered after waking. He always said they were blurry, obscure. But she knew they must be frightening. Sometimes he would dart up and stare off at the wall for seconds before realizing where he was. When she had witnessed it for the first time, after they moved here, it had startled her. Because before, in the rare cases she had stayed with him, it had never happened. Perhaps he had each time been too exhausted to even dream anymore. Or too engrossed in the feeling of them. In the fleetingness of these moments. And then faced the demons again alone. She closed her tired eyes. The thought now hurt.

Another shiver ran through him. His breathing became harsher, the tendons in his neck distinct. She fully rolled over, gently pushed him to move him back to his side. He groaned, something like a whine but when she slid her arm over his waist and pressed herself against his back, the trembling eased. She tucked her knees behind his, rested her forehead lightly between his shoulder blades and pressed the gentlest kiss against his sweaty skin. He was ice cold.

A moment passed before she felt him stir. Her hand got caught in a larger one, was brought to soft lips kissing her fingertips. She moved against him to itch closer, finding there was already no more space left. His voice was coated with sleep. “Did I wake you?” He turned carefully within her embrace until he was facing her. His hair clung damply to his forehead, but his eyes were mellow the moment they settled on her. She hesitated, then shook her head once. “No.” He raised a brow, interest visibly raised, and she sighed. She wasn’t in the mood for an interrogation.

Surprisingly though, he didn’t ask, combed his fingers through her hair instead. She allowed herself to shut her eyes. It felt like solace. Then his hand fell away and he bowed to place a kiss to her hairline. She reopened her eyes to find him smiling sleepily. It was completely silent around them, suddenly. So void of anything but his warmth. He kissed her brow, her nose, then her lips. She leaned in, kissed him fully and his legs tangled with hers. “You know” he murmured before letting his tongue flicker against hers once more “I might have an idea what could help a sleepless night.” She grinned, arched against him, hips finding his pelvis “Yeah?” she whispered. “And what could that be?” He chuckled, let her savor him for another long moment and then went still. He kissed her chastely and then got up. She frowned as she watched him leave the room, heard him descend the stairs shortly after.

He returned a few minutes later with streamy mugs in his hands. She propped herself up on her elbows. "Chamomile" he murmured as he climbed back beneath the blanket. “I always keep a package.” She nodded, smiled faintly. “Yeah. I remember.” And he smiled back at her. No bitterness. No shame. They held their gazes for a long moment before she sat up and leaned against the headboard beside him. He handed her the mug. She inhaled the warm scent, carefully sipped. His legs stretched out underneath the blanket, his ankle came to rest between her calves. There was nothing to be heard except the faint rusting of the sheets over their legs. Nothing she felt except his warm body beside her. Nothing that distracted her from the moment and the hot drink in her hand. She thought she could stay like this forever.

A shrill sound cut the bliss. She didn’t flinch, entirely controlling her body but her heart had started to hammer as she looked around to make out the source of the interruption. He let out a groan of frustration, held his mug toward her. She accepted it automatically, balancing both cups in her hands while he rubbed the sleep from his eyes. He reached for his nightstand to fetch his phone and looked at the display. His expression changed subtly. Like a breeze stirring quiet waters. Not a wave just yet, but already moving the tides. “Hunnigan” he greeted with a raspy voice. An unpleasant feeling spread within her stomach, chasing away the peace she had felt seconds before, replacing it with thunderclouds.

“Yeah.” She couldn’t hear what she was saying exactly. The tone she applied, however, already indicated that this was serious. It had to be considering the timing. “Confirmed?” he asked as she placed his mug on the nightstand beside her. It would go cold there. Its fate was inevitable at this point. “Okay. I understand.”

His voice was calm and professional. He was an experienced agent, he was brave, he wasn’t afraid. Not of missions. Not anymore. She didn’t know if there were other things that scared him, thought it had to be considering the previous nightmare. During a brief but intense encounter following one of his assignments in New York where the streets of the city had become a battlefield and civilians had paid the price, he had told her that he feared not getting somewhere in time. Not being ready, not being able to prevent people from getting hurt. When she had asked him why he felt responsible he had retorted a one lined platitude. That it came with the badge. And back then she had accepted it as an answer, had wanted him to keep fucking her, had wanted to make him to forget.

She turned her head to look at him. He glanced back at her only briefly before looking away again. He couldn’t stand it right now. He was avoiding her. He was falling and unwilling to show it. As if she hadn’t already seen. She had seen this man. Over and over again. She knew him by heart.

“I am at home” he said, and she just stared forward. From the corner of her eye, she watched him nod slightly. “I could be ready in about 15 Minutes.” After a few more words from the other end the call disconnected. For an instant he simply sat there, the phone still in his hand. The display illuminated. Some further instructions, she assumed. Slowly, she turned her head to face him. He met her gaze this time.

He looked exhausted, fatigue clinging to every feature. But not the slightest hint of grogginess reflected in his eyes. Their blue was piercing and icy sharp now. Whatever weariness had been there a heartbeat ago had retreated behind duty. In only about a minute, he had ceased to be a tired man and become precisely what years of relentless service had made of him. Not that the exhaustion hadn’t remained. It just simply no longer mattered. He threw the phone beside him on the mattress and then stood and went to his closet, assembling pieces of tactical gear. “The lead you gave me was good” he said while pulling a dark shirt over his head. “Hunnigan just confirmed it. A local DSO asset spotted the Umbrella researcher in Cape Town half an hour ago.” She just watched him, didn’t react. Wasn’t much of a surprise. Her sources rarely failed. He fastened the holster at his side, checked its retention almost absentmindedly before reaching for another pouch. “The contact person mentioned by your source is still there too” he continued. “Now we’ll have to check him on the ground. A team is being assembled in the field. We will verify whatever information he has and investigate the matter before those involved scatter and start cleaning up.”

He secured the straps beneath his arms. And then looked at her. She had remained seated on the bed, still sipping her tea. For the first time since the call, his movements slowed. Then he crossed the room to sit beside her, on the edge of the bed. "Are you going?" she asked, keeping her tone void of emotion. "Or are you running the operation from here?" He shrugged, eyes finding the floor. “I don’t know. Hunnigan only said to get to headquarters. But I’ve been overseeing this case for months, and I’m the one ultimately responsible for it. Guess we’ll figure things out once I get there.” She nodded. “Okay.”

He reached for her free hand, his fingers closing around it. He was still wearing the ring and didn’t look like he intended to take it off, as far as she knew hadn’t once since she had put it on him. “I love you. I hate to leave.” His eyes searched hers like he needed to make certain she understood before he walked out that door. Of course she did. It had never been different between them. It had never even been a question. Just for the first time it felt… Too abrupt. She didn’t know what to say when suddenly all she wanted was that he stayed. When she couldn’t even exactly say why, couldn’t tell what was different. She was not usually clingy. They were used to being apart. But perhaps the weekend, perhaps the recent weeks since her return where they had been together more than ever before because he had stayed with her more often, had worked remotely, perhaps silently refusing to let her out of his sight again. Maybe, she considered, that was the actual cruelty of addiction. It wasn't born from deprivation. It grew with every taste, every indulgence, until what had once been enough no longer came close. Anyway, it was now irrelevant. The assignment would not wait, and whatever had suddenly taken root inside her was hers to contend with, not his to carry. So she put on her most nonchalant expression, smiled at him, hoping her efforts to be sufficient to make it reach her eyes. "I made rounds for your magnum" she said, aiming for a matter-of-factly tone. "Explosive once fired. It's in the utility room." He blinked, caught off guard for the briefest instant. Then he smiled. A genuine one. Soft and arm.

"What would I do without you? " She gave the slightest shrug. “No need to entertain hypotheticals.” His smile went wide, his eyes went putty. Then he leaned in for a kiss. Which she fully returned, with no hesitation. When he broke away, he stayed close for another second, eyes locked on hers. He wanted to kiss her again. She could see it. Wanted to tell her to lock the door behind him, to stay inside, to not move an inch until his return. Instead, he simply nodded once. "You handed me the lead we've been missing. We would still be wandering blind without you.” He grinned faintly, a slight teasing glint reaching his eyes. “… and you made ammo for me. Might get the wrong ideas, you know?” She narrowed her eyes. “Like?” “Like we are actually becoming partners after all.” Entirely opting to ignore the message underneath, she just stared back at him, lips pressed into a thin line. “Don’t flatter yourself, Kennedy.” But as it seemed, he also hadn’t been looking for confirmation but had already made up his mind. Because he just kept smiling. “Too late.” It took her some effort now to maintain an unphased expression. The smile she suppressed pulled her throat tight. He thumbed her cheekbone once more then stood, picked up his phone, cast one last glance around the bedroom and then headed for the door.

She listened to his footsteps disappearing into the bathroom and after a couple of minutes down the hallway. A cupboard opened in the utility room and closed again with a thud. A few seconds later she heard the door swing shut downstairs. Pictured him being in the hallway right now heading towards the elevator. Gone but close enough she could still run after him. If she moved now, she would still catch him. But she remained motionless in the dark.

Then she darted up. Flew down the stairs to the utility room which’s door he had left open. She fetched the rifle, grabbed a handful of cartridges and fed them into the magazine as she hurried to the office overlooking the street. She opened the window, leaned out, carefully taking care she remained hidden in the shadows.

She rested the rifle against her shoulder and searched the street below through the scope. Only a mere seconds later he stepped out of the building and paused beneath the entrance canopy, hands buried in the pockets of his jacket. He remained there all the while she never for a second left him out of her aim. Then, suddenly three black vans rolled around the corner. Tinted windows. Antennas. Two drivers each. Government without a doubt, impossible for her to not notice. She narrowed her eyes, let out a quiet huff. Well. And neither for anyone else, for that matter. Really, DSO had no sense of discretion.

The vehicles stopped, the rear door of the middle one opened. She aimed straight, he moved into her line of fire. His back was to her, completely exposed. At this distance, if someone ambushed him, she could put a round through them before they ever reached him. The barrel pointed just past his right cheek, following him as he crossed the pavement. In case she needed to fire there was the not unlikely chance she would graze his beautiful face. She smiled softly. Would just be another scar. She loved every single one of them anyway. But he simply climbed inside after which the door slid shut and the convoy set itself back in motion to disappear into the night. Only when their taillights vanished did she lower her weapon. She leaned back, breathed in the cool night air and the faint oily scent of the rifle, closed her eyes as she held its smooth and heavy frame tight. Apparently, being the one left behind could hurt, at times.

Back in the bedroom she switched on the lamp on her nightstand. But she didn’t lie down, instead headed to her wardrobe. She searched for the pants she had worn on Saturday and, after fumbling briefly, pulled the ring he had given her out of its pocket. She had put it there when he had taken her to bed that night and not dared to remove it again since. But now she was alone. Now nobody was watching.

She went to sit at her bedside. The shiny piece of silvery metal was heavy, flashed slightly upon being turned towards the light. Its simplicity gave it a restraint elegance. It was solid, she didn’t think it could break. She rotated it between her fingers when a detail caught her eye. There was something on the inside, she noticed. She inspected it more closely. An engraving.

A rocket launcher. She lifted the piece to her eyes, but the verdict remained the same. Something tugged at the corners of her mouth but she didn’t quite manage a smile. It had looked like the one on the compact she had used for years and then at some point left with him to hide the chip with evidence that had proved his innocence. Did he still have it?

It didn’t look like it had been randomly chosen. The whole ring did not look randomly chosen. Rather very intentionally. She surveyed it again. The RPG-7. A weapon that had rescued him more than once. She had often used it to keep him alive.

Who looked out for him now? She thought about Cape Town. About the researcher, the local contract there she had referred him to. Should she have left it alone? If she had, would he still be here now? She closed her eyes for a moment. Perhaps. But even if that were the case, it would have been nothing more than a snapshot in time, wouldn’t it? The fact that he had to return to the field at some point had been inevitable. And suddenly it felt clear again, maybe clearer than ever, that keeping him safe would never be within her power. She would never be able to control his fate. When she despised chances.

Chapter 12: Surprise

Despite having taken a shower at the gym, he was still sweating a little when he entered the place. He dropped his bag beside the door and walked into the kitchen to get himself one of these gingers waters of hers he had grown so fond of. He opened the fridge, took one out and hastily consumed it in heavy gulps while he listened. It was strangely quiet. Perhaps she wasn’t home. He tried to remember if she had said something earlier but couldn’t come up with anything. Perhaps he hadn’t listened properly. He breathed in, noticed the faint scent of food in the air. He knew she liked these dumplings a takeaway down the street sold. She often bought them, just for herself, at times when he wasn’t home. She thought he didn’t notice. She had once brought some home when he had been present. He had eaten more than half of them and she had never made that mistake again. He smiled a little.

He pulled his phone from his pocket and dialed her number, heard a buzzing sound from the coffee table before the couch. He sighed. Great. There was just no way ever to get a grip on Ada Wong when she didn’t desire to be caught up with. His gaze fell on the clock. It was after five. Where the hell was she?

His glance fell on the small TV that hung over the kitchen countertop. He switched it on, sat down on one of the stools before the cooking island. He zapped through the channels then eventually paused at a cooking show. He leaned back as he watched a blonde woman explaining step by step how to make a perfect soufflé while a man in leather jacket who he assumed to be some kind of rockstar he had never heard of nodded ridiculously seriously beside her.

He watched their conversation about his new album and private habits with disinterest but leaned forward as the guy walked towards the oven to open it. He tensed. “Don’t” he muttered. The door was pulled open, the audience laughed. The soufflé collapsed. He rolled his eyes. Amateur. He couldn’t watch any longer after this.

But he was getting hungry and so he went to reopen the fridge to think about something he could make when the front door swung open and she appeared in the frame, packed with bags and wearing a smile that caused his heart to flutter, his lips curved up automatically. Her mood seemed exceptionally bright.

You are only running around in gym attire anymore.” He swallowed hard but before he could say something she had already focused back on her task.

He opted against socks, remained barefoot, tucked his shirt inside his pants and finished with a woven leather belt. He checked his appearance in the mirror, driving his hand through his hair to correct it one last time. Then made his way back downstairs.

He stared at her as the words sunk in. What day was it? Saturday. And July. He had no fucking clue which day exactly but Thursday had been the twenty-ninth so… It seemed right. And looking at her expression there also couldn’t be a doubt. She was not mistaken. Ada Wong didn’t do inaccuracies.

He hadn’t even remotely thought of it. He chuckled a little, helplessly dragged a hand over his face to hide from her. When had he last celebrated a birthday? He honestly wasn't sure. Perhaps in the orphanage. With small sweet cakes and candles and caretakers that had tried their best to make the children experience a nice day and some attention. Even back then he had always felt vaguely uncomfortable sitting at the center of a table while everyone watched. Not once after that. He didn’t even think anyone knew his birthday date. Well, except for Ingrid Hunnigan. Maybe. But she was way too discrete anyway to ever be doing something about it. And it also had never meant very much to him.

But suddenly, from one second to the other things felt different. Everything was different. Because now he was sitting across from the love of his life, from this attentive and prepared mercy of fate. His throat burned and for moment he felt like crying.

She noticed his torment, squeezed his hand tighter, nodded towards the food. “Try it. It should be eaten hot.” He took a shaky dissolved breath, reached for the tea and raised it to his lips with unsteady hands. It tasted so good that he felt himself smile but he still couldn’t speak, tended to the bowl instead.

The broth was warm and rich and soothing for the slight tremors in his body from his nerves. It tasted foreign and amazing and he took another mouthful. It was just so good. He wondered if his voice would give too much of his emotion away, decided to speak regardless. “This is… I don’t know, Ada. I don’t know what to say.” Her lips curved slightly, cautious, however. She didn’t speak. He vaguely nodded towards the table. “How am I supposed to deal with all of this? I feel… Overwhelmed.” Her brows tensed slightly. “As in nicely? Or not so much?” He locked gazes with her, saw a little very uncharacteristically uncertainty within them. Fuck. Had he made her doubt? She was usually so good at reading him that this slight display of irritation caught him by surprise. And made him reach for her second hand, too, until he held both of them firmly, his body leaning across the corner of the table where they sat beside each other. “You fucking kidding me? This is probably the most beautiful birthday anyone has ever given me.” He let out a huff. “Scratch the probably. This feels so fucking nice, Ada. Can’t believe you thought of it. I don’t even know what to say to you.” At that her smile returned and it was so infectious that he mirrored it instantly.

She looked at his food. “You must eat them, you know” she said quietly. For a moment he hesitated, waited, perhaps for her to continue, to explain. When she remained silent, though, he let go of her, picked back up his sticks and spoon.

The noodles seemed infinite. Every time he thought he had reached the end, more emerged from the broth. He looked up and she took a sip from her tea. He wanted to ask her a million questions. One of them, she answered automatically. “They symbolize a long life.” He lowered his sticks all the way down as her gaze settled on him. Sharing something of her with him because she knew how he longed to know these things and at the same time wordlessly telling him that she needed him to live. That she was wishing him many years. And sitting here, in heaven, surrounded by clouds, he assumed it would be happy ones. Not always stable, perhaps. With downsides whenever she would be away. But solid regardless, unshakably. He noticed her looking at him, felt so terribly sorry but he couldn’t focus on it now, didn’t waste a second. He would forever make this up to her but was out of the door immediately after he had muttered an incoherent apology.
