Apologies for it being so short. But it is an interlude to the final that would have been way too long otherwise. <3

Chapter 13: Gift

The coordinates led him just outside the city.

He drove fast while he unnecessarily kept half an eye on his telephone that he had thrown inside the center console. But it didn’t ring again after she had sent him the location. There was just utter silence except for his hammering pulse rushing in his ears.

She had sounded agitated. Stressed and urgent. He worked his mind to think of something that could have happened. And around here of all places. He had been fully prepared to request immediate extraction from DSO before he had discovered that the destination hadn’t been far. Thereafter he had just jumped in his car. And then in the haste not called anybody.

But now, on the road, he could afford the time to pause and think for an instant. And retrospectively things didn’t really align. The location… What she had said… Was it possible that this was a trap?

Couldn’t be ruled out. But what were the options? He couldn’t not go, could he? Shit perhaps he should have asked Ada for backup, he now thought. It was irresponsible running into a situation like this all alone. How old was he? Five? Why had he not considered it before? Perhaps because he was as dumb and reckless as she had always accused him of. If he still could, he promised himself to campaign for his own permanent release from duty after this. His stupidity was a liability.

“Call Ada” he sharply commanded the car. “Okay. I am calling Ada.” He impatiently tapped his fingers against the wheel as he waited for her to pick up. But the line didn’t connect. He cursed under his breath. “Fuck it, Ada” he hissed. In a situation like this, after he had left like this, how in hell could she not respond? What the fuck was wrong with this woman? Her voicemail eventually answered. He spat angrily. “I might need you for backup, so get the hell over here. Where the fuck are you anyway? I’ll share my location.”

A minute later he arrived, dropped his car on the fly and got out. The place before him was a bar. Looking perfectly ordinary as far as he could tell. Not abandoned at least. Just a building sitting beside a road. He drew his gun as he approached, aimed straight when the door opened.

It was Claire. He didn’t lower his weapon, stayed sharp. She looked alert. Strangely though, he noticed, she was wearing dark jeans and what looked like a fitted white blouse underneath her red worn leather jacket. She looked… So clean. So… Tidy? He frowned. "Finally" she said. He closed the remaining distance quickly, hyper aware of the surroundings. "What’s going on?" he asked, voice low but she didn’t answer, instead grabbed his arm, pulling him along.

His stomach dropped. Not exactly reassuring. "Claire" he hissed under his breath, letting his arm sink down at last, but she just dragged him forward. "Just come inside. Trust me.” His frown deepened. Those words had rarely improved any situation in his life. The moment he stepped inside he became even more suspicious. Noise hit him. Just not the way he might have expected.

He had expected screams, perhaps blood, violence, maybe B.O.W.’s. Not something that seemed like an average evening location. There were voices, music came from somewhere near the counter, laughter. The light over the worn wooden tables was yellow and dim, a television on a wall showed a football game. It smelled of fried food and used air and old lumber.

He bumped into Claire directly after the threshold, she had halted. He locked his confused gaze with hers and she, very slowly, reached for his gun to take it out of his hand. He let it happen, just stared, eyes wide, feeling completely at loss. She tucked the gun away in her jacket and guided him deeper inside.

His eyes wandered about, still somehow ready for anything. And then she turned around to him. To his utter disbelief, he realized that she smiled. “What the…" he started when he spacily glanced over her shoulder and saw the table. At the very back of the room, set behind a wooden divider. Before it stood Jill. Beside her Rebecca. Sherry. Chris. Helena. Ark. Ark? Ark fucking Thompson? What the hell? They were all looking so annoyingly cheerful and content. Most likely not least thanks to his current facial expression. He couldn’t move, could but stare as the adrenaline drained away, leaving only one realization.

Oh.

No. Please no.

The first thing he felt was regret. He appreciated them being here, but he would in every life and the next have preferred staying at home with her. Ada, shit. He needed to call her, needed to apologize, needed to… He thought. About the way she had encouraged him to answer the call. About the way she had suggested for him to put on some decent clothes. The clothes… Could it be that she hadn’t really rejected him earlier? That she had sent him to shower because she had known what would happen? It now seemed very likely. And so the second thing he felt was relief. It hit him like a slap. Then came remorse. Why in hell had she allowed this to happen? Him being taken away from her? On his fucking birthday.

He ran a hand through his hair, looked at the people before him again. Well fuck. Now he had to go through with this, he assumed. Maybe, if he acted particularly grateful, he would at least be let off the hook before midnight. He drew in a deep breath and approached the group.

Chris was laughing. He deeply hated him, considered firing into that smug expression after all. He vaguely recalled, however, that Claire had already disarmed him. Why again had he held Ada back the other day? But he couldn’t drown in regret because Jill stepped forward, ignoring his expression entirely and pulled him into her arms into a firm hug. “HBD Kennedy. Terrible forty. You are officially old now. Welcome to the club.”

He opened his mouth when she released him. Closed it. Opened it again. "Well thanks I… I thought somebody was dying" he heard himself mutter, still beside himself. Rebecca looked entirely apologetic now while Sherry started laughing. Chris still looked like Christmas had come early this year. He didn’t think he’d ever seen the man that cheerful. "Happy birthday Kennedy" he said, pulling him into another tight hug. “You look amazing. I would give everything to have it captured on camera.” He heard a familiar voice he hadn’t heard in a long time. “Perhaps they have surveillance. I’ll find out.” He looked in the direction from where it had come, and locked gazes with one of his oldest friends. He hadn’t seen him in quite a bit. He couldn’t believe he was here now. Who had invited him? For a second, he just stood, frozen, then looked at Sherry. She smiled, very softly. So she had reached out to him? How had she known? Had she been the one who had planned all this? For him?

It shattered his defenses. She looked so happy. And as the seconds ticked by, he suddenly couldn’t help but smile too. Still with a good deal of reluctance within it. But fuck it. For what it was worth. He drew in a deep breath as everyone approached him, one by one. More hugs, and friendly wishes, each one delivered in a mix of genuine affection and malicious joy. Which somehow sufficiently prevented him from remaining annoyed despite them having ruined what could have been his most perfect birthday in all history. He thought about her again. Please don’t leave me here. All alone..

By the time Claire hugged him he had fully accepted defeat. She kissed his cheek, long and lingering, arms tight around his shoulders before she pushed him away to look at him. Her eyes were an ocean of guilt. “I am so sorry, Leon.” He opened his mouth, waived dismissively with his hand but she continued before he could get a word out. “Not just about tricking you… But also about last time. About the hard time I gave you…” she looked to the floor. “And her. The things I said… It was just…” He cut her off, reached for her hand, held on tight. “It’s okay. All of it. You don’t owe me an apology. Quite on the contrary. I should thank you instead.” She frowned. “Oh. Not at all. Honestly, it was all Sherry’s idea.”

He huffed, rolled his eyes clicking his tongue. “I wasn’t talking about this here. I meant for trusting me. I know you haven’t told Chris. I will not forget that, Claire.” She held his gaze as she nodded faintly. Then, suddenly she put her head against his collarbone and wrapped her arm around his waist to pull him close. He stiffened briefly from surprise but then relaxed, placed his arm over her shoulders. “I shouldn’t have doubted you in the first place” she muttered and for a moment they simply stayed like this before she continued. “I do trust you, Leon. Really. And… Well. From what I hear… Is she the one?” He inhaled, throat suddenly dry, held her a little tighter. “Yes. She is. She has always been.” For a moment they remained like this, neither saying anything.

“Sherry told me she is also very nice.” She looked up at him and he looked down to meet her gaze, heartrate picking up considerably at hearing what Sherry had apparently said about her. “I know I totally messed up last time but… Do you think she would be up for giving me another chance?” He chuckled, allowed her to hold him for a moment longer as he thought it through. He believed she absolutely would. But he just huffed. “Who knows Claire. She can be… Unpredictable. But…” and an inward grin tucked on his lips at that, as he thought about the sweetest of all memories, so precious that he had locked it in his box for all eternity “… she might be. If you ask her nicely.”

She nudged him in the ribs and he could tell she was on the verge of retorting something when her brother stepped close. He glared at the man. "You really had me there." He chuckled, lit a cigarette and blew the smoke in their faces. "I know. Was a delight." Traitor. Complete traitor. And yet as he looked at his friends, Claire wrapped up in his arms, Chris’ bright expression, he felt something unexpected, something not distinctly definable. Perhaps warmth. These people had most likely crossed countries, had fit tonight into their schedules. Scared the shit out of him. Screwed his evening to hell. All for this. For his fucking birthday.

She pulled the car over and halted beside the street. A little farther down the road she saw the bar Sherry had told her to come to. It looked unspectacular, plain but well busy. Occasionally, cars got dropped before it, people crossed the parking lot to enter. She watched. She knew she was supposed to go inside. It was his birthday. Most likely everyone was expecting her. And yet she couldn’t move, had chosen to park in the distance in order not to be seen.

She stared out of the windshield until her vision blurred, hands still firmly gripping the wheel. She felt like her body weighted four hundred pounds.

He reached for his coke and her head turned towards him. He picked up her gaze, gave her a lazy smile. “Claire told me you were responsible for all this” he accused softly, leaning towards her ear. He briefly considered adding a complaint, too, but bit his tongue instead, forbidding himself any further remark. She had meant well. He knew she had done it to make him happy. To show him they all cared.

She smiled, searching his eyes, shrugged. “Yeah, uhm, kind of. But…Not all credit belongs with me. I couldn’t have done it without a certain someone’s assistance.” He lifted his brows. “That so?” She nodded. “Yeah. I called her and asked if it was okay if I came over this weekend… You know, I have never been to Boston and I thought it might be nice to spend some time, and…” He placed his hand over hers, squeezed gently. “You don’t need a reason for coming over, Sherry. You are always welcome. I think…” and he really thought about it for a moment, wondering if he would be lying and coming to the conclusion that he honestly didn’t think so “… I think Ada feels the same way.” She chuckled, clutched his palm a little tighter. “I know. She was really sweet.” She regarded him. “She said it would actually be perfect timing. Because it would be your birthday that Saturday.” He now frowned, as Sherry looked him deeply in the eyes. “I haven’t even thought about it before she mentioned it. I was completely unaware. It made me realize that… We have never once celebrated one of your birthday’s. You never even told me the date. You never talked about it in all these years. I kind of always felt like you would find something like birthday congratulations annoying. You never seemed like you wanted the attention. Or any attention for that matter. You… You are quite a good listener. I never found it hard to talk to you. I always had the feeling that I could rely on you. Whenever I called you, you were there. No questions asked. But despite your easy nature you are actually pretty reserved when it comes to the things that matter to you personally. Everything I ever knew about you I had to press out.”

His eyes found the table before him, he chuckled a little self-consciously, pulled his hand away to scratch the back of his head. “Yeah well… Guess I am not that interesting for starters…” She shook her head. “It’s not that, Leon. We both know it. I think it’s rather that you simply don’t like to open up towards others. You don’t like to show the parts that are not an agent, not armed, not hard and sarcastic and numb. Claire believes so too.” He swallowed, stared, felt caught despite not even knowing for what. So yeah, maybe he didn’t exactly enjoy talking about things. So fucking what. Not everyone could be as oversharing as her or Claire, could they?

Her expression softened. “Don’t get me wrong. You have every right to be who you are. Obviously. You can sort things out on your own. We have always respected that and of course it doesn’t make us like you any less. Still…” she briefly gazed sidewards, fumbling with her earring “… it kind of used to worry us. But… you know what?” He briefly glanced at the ceiling, becoming slightly unnerved. It caused a little exhaustion to infiltrate his tone. “What?” “I don’t find it concerning anymore.” He watched her expression brighten considerably. “Because I know you have her now. Someone who knows things. Like your birthday. Someone you apparently let in.”

All annoyance about unbidden self-reflection aside, he considered what she had said for a moment. And he assumed she was not wrong. He did not like to share things about himself. Because he didn’t think it was anyone else’s concern and maybe because… They all had their individual weight to carry. Their issues to face. He wouldn’t have wanted to burden them with his own struggles too. It was easier to be strong than admitting weaknesses.

In sharp contrast to that, he had not felt the need to hide anything from Ada from the first moment on. She was the strongest person he had ever encountered. And there had also never been any need to pretend because the first thing she had seen of him had been a boy with one foot in the grave, completely lost in an infected police station. Someone who had more stumbled along beside her than anything else, someone with more luck than skill.

He had only become the survivor after. The soldier. The special agent. And somehow… It had always given him some strange kind of comfort around her. She had already seen him at his lowest. And underneath it all he knew that this had also been his reason for falling in love with her. Not her looks. Not the sex. Not because she had saved him. But because she had always known who he was. And it had at all times made it so fucking easy for him to open up towards her. To tell her about missions, and pain, and the feeling of incapability. And thinking of it now, he realized that, despite not being constantly around or maybe even exactly because of it, with her he had shared all of it. She been the one he had confided in when things had mattered. Because he had never minded her seeing him vulnerable. She had always already known he was. The one under whose blanket he had crawled when he hadn’t been able to cope with withdrawal anymore.

He looked at Sherry and smiled, despite himself, took a sip from his drink. “Huh. For someone complaining about me being too closed off, you seem to know me pretty well.” She giggled rolled her eyes. “Yeah. I am an experienced agent. I read between the lines.” He huffed a breath through his nose when his attention got caught by Claire, who leaned over the table towards them. “Hey what are you whispering about all the time? I’d hate missing out on gossip.” Sherry laughed and he leaned back. He sighed. “Women.”

Eventually she left the car and locked it up. She crossed the street, walked a few meters until she reached the parking lot and then made her way towards the entrance with unusually tentative steps. The gravel would ruin her heels. A sigh escaped.

She fully paused before the fly of stairs that lead to the porch, drew in a deep breath. And then didn’t ascend. Instead, she walked around the building, maintaining a safe distance, to peak through the windows. She spotted them after a moment. They were sitting around a table in a corner. Most of them she knew, one man she had never seen.

She surveyed Leon. His posture was relaxed, his smile was easy, boyish kind of… He looked cute without trying. Happy.

Everybody looked easy, she found a minute later. Redfield was leaned back in his chair and… Oh. Jill Valentine sat beside him. The corners of her mouth tugged slightly upwards before she forced her eyes away. Sherry sat close enough to Leon to occasionally touch his arm when she spoke. And Chambers was there too. She hadn’t seen her again after she had looked after her bullet wound almost two years ago. She didn’t know if she knew about her and Leon. With any other group of friends she’d assumed yes. With them, however, she couldn’t be sure. Then she saw Helena Harper. It made her smile before she remembered their first encounter in Tall Oaks. Where she had shot her sister. Straight in her head. The smile froze. She had no idea what she had thought about her then. But she had never seemed hostile towards her. Perhaps largely thanks to the fact that, by protecting him, she had at the same time helped her out once or twice as well.

The unfamiliar man with brown hair looked about Leons age. He sat beside Claire, they seemed deeply in conversation and for an instant she wondered if it was her boyfriend. But, paying closer attention, she didn’t think it was the case. They didn’t seem particular familiar with each other.

She stood, immobile. The air around her still felt warm. It was a nice summer night. She knew she should go inside. It was ridiculous. But standing here on the outside, looking in it felt… Easier. Familiar. She had so often watched them from the distance without ever coming too close. Leon had for the longest time been her only exception. And it had changed lately, she was aware of it, she had gotten to know some of them recently. But still it felt more natural to remain within a distance. She didn’t belong within a circle like them. With any circle, for that matter.

And so she just couldn’t bring herself to move. And then suddenly Sherry’s head turned slightly. She noticed immediately, evaluated her options of hiding but a second later she knew it was already too late. Sherry narrowed her eyes as she looked through the window and their gazes met. She blinked. And watched Sherry rise. She inhaled and started walking. Since she was already coming for her, why not meet her halfway.

They met before the stairs. And Sherry smiled. “Hi there, Ada.” She forced a smile as well. “Hey.” There was a brief pause, a moment’s hesitation. And then Sherry opened her arms and wrapped them around her. She couldn’t quite return the embrace, felt stiff, was released after a second. Sherry’s expression remained very warm, however, maybe now slightly apologetic. “I am glad you are here” she said with a look that was all sincere. She stared back at her, couldn’t say the same thing. And somehow, she thought she knew, somehow, she looked as if she understood. Sherry glanced towards the door. “He will be very happy, Ada.”

She nodded faintly. She knew he would and yet… She trailed off, couldn’t finish the thought as Sherry took another step towards her, picked up her gaze. She allowed it, lifting her eyes to lock them on hers. “I imagine it is not easy. First times never are. But… You are not alone in this.” She spoke more quietly now, more guarded. “You have more than just one friend in there, you know” she said.

She didn’t answer, didn’t look away. Then she directed her gaze to the floor. More than just one friend in there. She had never thought about it but was Sherry… her friend? To her surprise, she found herself unable to dismiss the thought entirely. Sherry was looking at her for who she really was now. The mask had fallen. Nothing seemed to be standing between them anymore. And she had come outside here without a moment’s hesitation. So maybe they could actually be friends. Someday.

Sherry turned towards the entrance, shot her another glance over her shoulder. She didn’t reach again, didn’t try to pull her along. “So… You coming?” And after a second, she followed. “Okay, Atta girl.”

He glanced towards the entrance, wondering where Sherry had gone, as he listened to Arks detailed narration about his most recent divorce. He’d had no idea, but the man had just revealed to them that he had apparently been married for three times already. Something almost impossible for him to comprehend. Or maybe his life simply wasn’t long enough for three marriages when getting hopelessly stuck on one woman had already taken most part of his adult years.

His eyes wandered and he desperately tried to suppress a smile as Ark explained how his third marriage had ended. Apparently after another long stretch of absences, broken promises and the sort of arguments that started over something small and somehow ended with one person packing a suitcase. The sort that he didn’t think could ever happen between him and her. Certainly, they’d had their fair share of things to work out. But behind closed doors and difficult conversations and missions aside, being with her had never felt anything but… Well. Smooth maybe. She had never judged, she had never treated him like a child, she had never tried to make decisions for him. In his deficits, she complemented him and there was nothing he could think of that he wanted from a life with someone that she didn't already give him. Not everything was easy, perhaps there were still plenty of challenges to overcome. But he sincerely believed they could work things out whatever they were. Like the fact that she wasn’t present right now.

He sighed. He understood and still found it sad. He wanted her with him. Wanted to show her off like some fucking lovesick idiot, wanted her warmth beside him, wanted all the little remarks and teases and smirks and disapproving looks.

His eyes suddenly caught a movement and he lifted his gaze to see Sherry returning. And then his breath got stuck in his throat. Because she was walking behind her. He blinked, stared. Warmth spread through his entire body from head to toe. His heart started to pound. She was here. She had actually come. There was something reserved in her expression, something not entirely relaxed and he would have wanted to jump to his feet and cross the room and pull her against him. Wrap himself up around her to shield her from whatever made her wary.

But he knew it would likely make things much worse for her. She wasn’t the one for public displays of affection. And she could handle herself. She didn’t need him to do it for her. And for the first time, he thought he would be able to simply let her be. He didn’t need to prove anything about them to anymore. So he just gave her a smile and then glanced at Sherry who smiled as well. “I brought another friend” she said with the slightest hint of pride shimmering in her irises and all heads turned in her direction.

He glanced towards the people at his table, noticed Rebecca’s eyes widening. She looked at Ada, then at him, then back at Ada again with an expression that very clearly asked what the fuck she had missed out on. Jill's brows rose, curiosity immediately replacing whatever conversation she'd been having. Chris, meanwhile, didn’t react nearly as reserved as he would have expected him to. He grinned, leaning back in his chair, regarding the scene.

Ark was the only one who seemed entirely undeterred by the sudden shift in attention. He pushed himself to his feet, nearly caught his chair with his leg, recovered and extended a hand toward her. “Hi. Ark Thompson.” He paused, glanced briefly at him as though remembering something before he reverted back to her. “I'm an old friend of Leon. And… uh… Single again, by the way. You arrived just im time for the end of the story.”

He closed his eyes, covered them underneath his hand as the whole table burst into laughter. Ark laughed, too, not at all minding the amusement at his expense and he couldn’t help a chuckle of his own escaping. Then he dropped his hand and looked at her properly. At last.

She had changed, wore a very short black leather skirt and a black silk blouse. She looked a lot like she had that time he'd found her in his bathroom with a bullet wound. But she didn’t feel much like that woman anymore. Because now she was here for him. Now he knew where he stood with her. Now she was wearing his ring. Perhaps not legalized by authorities but by every right one person could have to another. He was looking at his wife. His wife. So why again was he sitting here, glued to his chair, like a complete fool? She had said yes to him. Was showing up. And it was his birthday, wasn’t it? So why in hell would he not welcome her properly?

We are past this. And so he decided to do what felt natural after all. He pushed his chair back, stood and when she stepped within his reach, caught her waist and leaned down to press a gentle kiss to her lips. Brief. But with closed eyes and sweet unlike anything else. On the reverie, he noticed the laughter subsiding and felt all eyes bore into his back. But he didn’t care. Because when he pulled back, he saw only her. He gave her an apologetic smile and, never leaving his eyes, she simply smiled back. “Hello you” she greeted, eyes bold now as ever. Any and all concerns erased. He chewed on his lower lip before he answered. “What took you so long?”

Everything, perhaps. Everything she was. Everything she had been. Everything she would never be. She hated this.

But her heart was beating. And she loved him. So very much. And he stood between her and the table with his broad shoulders. So what was there to fear really? What was there that couldn’t be endured?

She held his icy blue gaze for a second before she glanced behind him, at the people looking at her. She made eye contact with Rebecca Chambers and received a little cautious smile. She returned it and for an instant they just looked at each other, and she wondered what Rebecca was thinking. She didn’t look like she had known. It made her wonder what Leon had told her back in the days. Probably not much.

And then she got distracted by someone standing up and when she looked she found it was Helena. She came over, extended her hand. Leon moved out of the way and she accepted it. “Good to see you again. And… uhm…” with that she looked to him who still stood close “… good to see you two. Together. Apparently.” His voice was very low as he answered. “Thank you.”

A second later the scraping of a chair being pushed over the floor caused their heads to turn. Claire had risen to her feet. Their eyes met as Claire stepped to the side, hand remaining on the back rest of the chair. Her expression was… Cautious. But not hard as it had been during their last encounter.

“Hi” she said softly. She regarded her. The reddish-brown hair was gathered into what seemed like her signature ponytail. And there suddenly was something in the shape of her face that made her eyes linger. A resemblance to Chris, she realized. It wasn’t so obvious, but there was something recognizable in her eyes, in the set of her jaw. Or it was just the stubbornness underneath the careful expression. Now that she knew both of them from a closer range though, things seemed to become a little clearer. A smile on her face formed all by itself.

“You can have my seat” Claire said and her eyes flickered to the chair and then back to her. There hadn’t been any undertone. She looked entirely sincere. It had been the offer to sit with him. And it somehow touched her, it was very kind or… Considerate. But actually, looking at all of them, into their curious but not at all dismissive expressions, she found that it really wasn't necessary. Their paths had crossed so many times already, in so many places and under the wildest circumstances. And seeing Rebecca here, Helena, Sherry. It made her feel less like an intruder.

At the same time, she didn’t want to appear rude, didn’t want to depreciate her gesture. She wondered about a way to put it when a very familiar voice addressed her. “Why don’t you just sit with us, Ada?” Her eyes found Rebeccas, who nodded towards a vacant seat opposite to her, beside where Helena had just left. Chris and Jill sat at that end of the table too. “I would simply love to catch up.” For an instant her gaze locked with Chris’s, and she thought she’d seen the ghost of anticipation in his eyes. Then she nodded before she reverted to Claire.

“It’s very kind of you” she said, tinting her voice carefully soft. “But you should keep it.” Claire held her gaze for a moment, then gave a small nod. “Okay.” She returned her faint smile before turning toward Rebecca. “I'd be happy to.” She stepped around Leon, and, as she passed him, let her fingers briefly brush the back of his hand. Rebecca shifted slightly to make room for her and she took a seat.

She let her eyes travel around the table. Chris was watching her, though there seemed to be no suspicion in his expression. She wondered how far he had come with Louisiana. Then the woman beside him caught her eyes. She offered her hand across the table, had perceptive blue eyes.

“Hi. I don’t think we have met, have we? I am Jill.” She took it and noticed it being soft. And, for some reason she couldn’t tell, it slightly surprised her. “Ada.” Jill’s brows rose and she cracked a smile that looked so easy that she thought she understood why it was capable of drawing a man like Chris Redfield. There was something about her and she found herself holding her gaze before Helena leaned slightly toward her.

“Has been some time” she smiled. “Yeah.” Four years to be precise. Four years that had changed everything. Four years of running and realizing some things couldn’t be left behind, no matter how hard one tried. “So” Helena murmured “I think we have quite a bit to catch up on.” Her eyes followed the slight nod in Leon’s direction. And he noticed immediately, his gaze finding hers across the room. His mouth curved before he turned back to the conversation around him. Hers did too, as she looked back at Helena.

She arched a brow, more playful than daring. “Is there? Because to me it rather seems like there’s no news at all.” Helena’s laughter made her chuckle too. “You know” she said, studying her for a moment. “I think that might actually be the most reassuring answer you could have given me.”

At some point she excused herself, to catch some fresh air. Even though she couldn’t say that talking to them had been exactly difficult, she still needed a moment. And only when she stepped outside, away from the light and into the darkness, it felt like she could properly breathe again.

Situations like this still challenged her. She was happy for him, she wasn’t indifferent towards the people, hell she thought she even liked them. Each in one or the other way. But she still didn’t feel like she entirely belonged with them. Or in the light. Because this wasn’t her socializing for a job. This wasn’t her playing a role and saying the right things. This was just… Her being… Her. The woman on Leon Kennedys side. The woman they had encountered somewhere along the way through him. A woman they now recognized. Someone who had lost their disguise.

So outside here, where the shadows spread their alluring shields, she felt safer. Even though deep down inside she knew that it had already become an illusion. She averted her gaze from the bar to look around the environment.

After a few minutes she heard soft cracking in the gravel. A second later the faintest scent of smoke reached her lungs. Her lips tugged slightly. “You shouldn’t sneak up to the villains, Redfield” she said softly, listened to the sound of approaching footsteps.

His massive frame appeared beside her. He didn’t look at her. “Thought I’d find you here. Lurking in the darkness.” She chuckled. “It has become more of a rarity than I would like to admit” she said, heard the faintest huff of air from him. “And even here it seems like I cannot have a single moment to myself.” He turned to look at her. “It might interest you to hear that we were able to extract two civilians in Louisiana” he said, entirely ignoring her previous remark. “I just returned a couple of days ago.”

She blinked. Two?

“Oh? Is that so?” He nodded, took a long drag from his cigarette, never leaving her gaze. He blew out the smoke while he spoke. “Mia and Ethan Winters. They are both alive and fine.” She carefully avoided the reflex to tense her brows, remained entirely blank instead. Ethan Winters? Her husband? So, she had reached him after all. She would have liked to smile. But hadn’t she been infected? Had she been mistaken in this regard? She doubted that. Strange. But he had said civilians. Alive and well. She looked at him.

“Who was behind it?” she asked innocently. He regarded her. “Lucas Baker had an underground lab. I pursued him as he tried to escape into the wetlands. Turned out he was an informant of a syndicate. The Connections. Ever heard of?” So Lucas fucking Baker had betrayed his family? The creep with the tools? Who would have thought. He had looked around for her, unsuccessfully, and about to check outside he stumbled into Ark, standing at the bar to which they had meanwhile relocated, a few paces away from the group, however. He was sipping a beer, looking at the dance floor.

Then he had dragged him all the way into his whole mess. He didn’t think Ark had ever recovered from it. He had retreated from what he had seen, had disappeared from his life piece by piece afterwards.

He, himself, had gone the other way. Deeper and deeper into the rabbit hole until there had been no way back. And perhaps his guilt towards his friend had stopped him from ever holding on too tightly. He had allowed him to let go. Of him. Of everything connected with bio terrorism.

Nothing will ever change that.” He nodded, a lump building in his throat as memories of two boys who had lost their parents flooded his mind. One boy’s path had led him to the military. The drinking.” He swallowed, chewed his cheeks. Talking about it still felt hard. Even with a person who a younger version of himself had shared the most intimate secrets with. “It was bad. From someone like you.” He blinked, frowned. “Someone like me?” “Yeah. You always seemed like a rock to me. After Sheena… Well. I was shattered. I still dream about the things I’ve seen. After all these years.” He inhaled, shook his head faintly. “Well. Looks like self-perception and how others perceive us can be extremely diverging.”

For a long moment they held each other’s gaze. Then Ark nodded, huffed a laugh. “And still you always manage to come out on top.” He chuckled, a little warily, thinking about his life until here, the many defeats, the many losses. Like shit I am.”

Ark’s gaze turned somber, maybe tinted with the hint of… Regret? He looked away before he started to speak. “No, I am serious. You have people around you who organize birthday parties for you. You have…” Ark huffed, rolled his eyes “… the goddamned prettiest woman I have ever seen. You obviously have been through much more than I have.” He snorted, briefly letting his head fall back to stare at the ceiling. Yeah, right. As if anything had ever simply fit with Ada. Because for all the mess it had taken to get here, he knew that what he had with her now was the closest to fulfillment he would ever reach in his life. So he waited out the silence.

Ark stared at him with the weight of the world. “Can I ask you a question?” He furrowed his brows, shrugged. “Sure.” He didn’t really know what to prepare for though. “Why don’t you simply let all of it go? You seem happy. You say you quit drinking. Why don’t you simply make her a perfect baby? With your sappy blue eyes and her flawless dark hair.” Ark chuckled and he stared as he felt heat climb his cheeks. A soft shaky breath left him, followed by a soundless, disbelieving laugh. And for an instant he felt something within him, something warm, something uncharted, something entirely foreign.

Then he shook his head muttered “I don’t think that this would even be possible anymore.” He couldn’t go down that road. It was out of the question. His face was still burning, however. Thank God the light was scarce around here.

Ark stared at him for a long moment. “And if it was?” He opened his mouth and closed it again. He honestly had never thought about it before. Not a single time.

“I don’t know” was the only answer he had. Ark studied him, then gave a faint shrug. “Okay. I’m not saying you need to have a kid or make a little Kennedy family to turn your life into a fucking greeting card. I am just saying that perhaps you should consider your priorities. Because I can tell you from my lifelong and also quite recent experience that the people in your life don’t wait for you to finally put them first.”

He lowered his eyes. Couldn’t help but smile. Because about this one thing he was certain. She knew what she was to him. And in case she ever forgot, he would never grow tired of reminding her. Because what he valued more than anything about her was the fact that with her, there had never been any need to explain anything about the world he lived in. They lived on common ground, they carried matching scars. She knew what he had seen, what life had made of him and what he was capable of doing when there was no other choice. And he knew her just as well. Perhaps not all the details, not all the little stories he was dying to hear. But who she was beneath the illusion. And he knew that whatever the future held for them, he would know where he would stand. Right beside her.

It made him reconsider what Ark had said. Perhaps things had fallen into place for him. Perhaps he was just that damn lucky bastard Ark believed he was. He looked at his old friend again. “Look at you, disappearing for years just to come back with life advice.” They both chuckled and he emptied his bottle in a long gulp before he added. “But I’ll keep it in mind.” And he feared he actually would. Every word.

She gently pulled a chair back at the table in the corner. Some unfinished drinks still stood here, left behind. They had relocated to the bar, the place had increasingly gotten crowded as the evening had progressed. Some people were on the dancefloor, some were still occupying tables. But most of the guests were standing. She didn’t think anyone would see her sitting here.

She watched the scene for a moment. Watched couples dancing, watched friends make silly moves, watched people glancing at each other. There was a blond man holding a beer, balance shaky. He stood with his back to her but she could tell he was drunk. Girls were giggling, looking in his direction. She wondered how long it would take until he trembled and humiliated himself completely. Probably not long. She wondered if it could have been him. Fifteen years ago or so. It ached. Still. Because she had not been there.

Another man caught her eye. Most likely ten years her junior. He had dark short hair, smiled when he noticed her looking. She averted her eyes and then blinked in surprise. Jill suddenly stood there, in the middle of the crowd. And for an instant, as their gazes met, she paused. But then she came over.

“Seems like I wasn’t the only one with this idea.” She smiled faintly. Shook her head and said nothing. Jill nodded towards a chair. “Mind if I joined you?” She didn’t know if she did. Gave her a “No” as an answer, however. Jill sat down, beckoned a waiter over and ordered two beers. She didn’t object. For a long time, they just sat side by side, both watching the people before them. When the beers arrived, Jill put one before her and kept the other for herself. She lifted the bottle. “Salute.” She didn’t pick hers up but inclined her head in Jill’s direction. “Salute.”

Jill drank in silence. After a moment she gestured with her bottle towards the primed guy who was currently dancing up to one of the girls of the group that had been standing around him. “What’s your bet? Will he fall by himself or will one of the boys over there put him in his place?” She sighed, surveyed a couple of young men who were watching as well. She sincerely hoped neither would happen. But him falling seemed the more lenient option.

“Ten dollars that he falls before this song ends.” She didn’t look at Jill but could hear amusement in her voice. “Okay. Then I am betting ten dollars on the boys.”

The man swayed dangerously, his drink dripping, entirely oblivious to the fact that the girl before him was trying to put some distance between them. He stumbled, one of the boys in the other group tensed. But then suddenly, another man emerged from the crowd. He grabbed the booze head by his arm, said something to him and, after what looked like futile protest, guided him away. They stared after them. Her chest tightened.

Then they looked at each other, exchanging a quiet smile. Jill took another sip, never leaving her gaze. “So… Any particular reason for hiding here?” She huffed, shrugged. “Could ask you the same thing.” Jill tilted her head. “Touché.” For a moment she seemed deep in thought. Then she continued. “Guess I just needed a break. I mean… I love to see everyone but… “ she trailed off, shook head, didn’t finish.

She regarded her before she answered. There was a heaviness about her she had not noticed before. “Yeah. I think I know what you mean.” After a brief silence, Jill looked at her again. “Chris told me about his visit to your place in Boston.” She nodded faintly, braced herself for being questioned about Simmons, about Raccoon. She felt her expression freeze into stone.

“He said that you are good for Leon.” She blinked, brows lifting somewhat automatically. She hadn’t expected this. And it took her a moment to process it. So, Chris Redfield had said something positive about her? To the person she expected he trusted most in his life? Perhaps apart from his sister.

Her gaze went towards the bar almost subconsciously. Redfield stood with the others, didn’t seem very engaged though, was rather looking about. She nodded in his direction. “Speaking of. He seems to be looking for someone.” Jill followed her gaze. Then she seemed to search for something. When she found it, she held it up. A blue, flattened papery pack. “Or something. I have his cigarettes.” They shared a smile. And she kept her gaze locked on the woman beside her. Decided that, since her boundaries had blurred enough over the past months, why not cross another line. At her own choice, for a change. “I don’t think it’s the cigarettes.” It earned her a frown. She lowered her eyes to the table before them, felt her lips curve slightly upward without her being able to prevent it.

When she looked back up, she found Jill still staring at her, almost blankly. Interesting. Did she not know? “I think he likes you a lot” she clarified quietly. Jill looked in his direction, sighed then. “Yeah, guess he does. We have been partners since Raccoon City.” She shot her a careful glance. “But… I assume you are aware of that.” She didn’t react to it, didn’t comment. Jill held her gaze for a long time before she continued. “I was abducted a couple of years ago. Ever since I returned….” she shook her head absentmindedly, searching for the right words or perhaps even wondering whether to keep talking at all “…. shit. I don’t know. Nothing has been the same after. He doesn’t treat me like a partner anymore. Rather like someone who needs to be supervised. Or worse. Protected.” They looked at each other and somehow, she thought, she felt something from her, something like… Like not hesitation exactly but maybe… Like being somehow of two minds about what she was supposed to say to her. Then she huffed a laugh, shook her head.

“And I hate being told what to do. Also, I don’t need protection. I can fucking handle myself.” Her tone had become hard, she now looked annoyed. She surveyed her as she rolled her eyes. “Sorry. I assume none of it is your problem.” She smiled, decided to not leave her with nothing, despite herself. Simply because she thought they weren’t so different. And because she found that maybe that could be enough for her to be able to like her.

“Well. In fact, all of it is. Precisely actually.” Jill gave her a deep frown. “Excuse me?” Her smile deepened. “I too hate being told what to do. I don’t need protection either, can handle myself perfectly.” Jill let out a little breathless chuckle, expression easing again. “How’s Leon dealing with it?” She shrugged. “He is dealing with it. Sometimes more, sometimes less successfully.” And then another smile passed between them. The second. Or the third? She’d lost count. It hadn’t happened in a very long time. Perhaps never. Except with him. Jill studied her, glancing at her untouched beer afterwards. And, keeping her eyes fixated on hers, she reached for it after a moment, slowly, and exchanged it with her empty bottle. She simply received it. Didn’t speak. Jill didn’t either. Instead, she took another long gulp.

From across the room Chris suddenly seemed to have spotted them and he waived and started approaching, squeezing himself through the other guests. Jill looked at her once more. “Addiction calling.” She just smiled. Thought she was not wrong. However, not the way she might have meant.

After Chris had lit a cigarette, he asked them to join him and the rest at the bar. She would have preferred to remain where she was. But then followed Jill and him as they started walking regardless.

Claire turned around to her, made space. Sherry smiled, Rebecca spoke of something. She didn’t really listen, didn’t really contribute. And still didn’t feel on the outside either. Leon wasn’t there, perhaps in the bathroom. She didn’t know, she had lost sight of him. But the space beside her was not empty. Jill Valentine stood there and occasionally they exchanged a glance as she casually started following a conversation about Rebecca’s experience with dating apps. Not without interest. And, as it seemed, Claire had quite a bit to contribute here.

Letting her gaze wander for an instant, she spotted Leon by the bar at last, standing with the man she now knew was named Ark Thompson. She still had no idea who he was though, decided she would ask him sometime.

She returned her attention back to the group, heard them debate over blind dates, over vacation flings and she registered how Sherry didn’t say a word to any of it. In contrast to Rebecca and Helena who, at some point stated that she believed that developing something real in their line of work was an outright impossibility. She was painfully aware of the stolen glances in her direction afterwards.

They switched to training routines and sports and then cars. And, listening to them, she realized that their conversations were dominated by quite common things, not shallow, but not entirely deep either. And looking around, wondering why that was she thought if not all of them had their own package to carry, their own parts they liked to keep quiet about. Like Sherry loving Albert Wesker’s son. Like Chris secretly loving Jill Valentine. Like Jill still struggling with her past. Like Leon giving a ring to someone who had once been their enemy. Neither seemed to appreciate surrendering depths. And still they were connected. And perhaps, she thought, she could actually fit somewhere in between. Never within the circle, she also did not desire that. But perhaps like spheres allowing their orbits to touch. Not as an elusive spy operating for the highest bidder. But as a Chinese woman who loved an American agent.

Her attention drifted to him again. To the way he stood at the bar, one shoulder turned to Ark as they spoke. He was beautiful when he talked, when he moved his full lips, when he shifted his weight by a gentle sway of his hips. He was so fucking gorgeous. Finger licking good. Claires’ deep chuckle pulled her attention back. But she could still feel the electricity of his eyes that she knew had stayed with her. She didn’t even need to check.

“You do realize you can’t keep going like this forever, do you Jill?” She frowned, realized she had lost track of what they had been talking about. But Jills expression looked deeply sad. It caused her brows to furrow, her posture to stiffen. “I’ve had it for so long. So many memories are tied to it.” Chris, too, looked lost in thought. Hurt, as well, in a way. It irritated her. “But you said it yourself. It keeps breaking down. It’s a risk. What if it happens in traffic?” She inhaled. So, they were talking about a vehicle? She relaxed slightly as Jill smiled ruefully. “Well. I’ve handled worse, I guess.” Claire rolled her eyes. “Yeah, exactly! No need to additionally challenge fate. Life is dangerous enough. Even without you driving around in broken cars. At least as long as you keep doing things like crawling into prison sewers all by yourself.”

Jill shot her a look. “Wow. No idea why you are bringing it up again. But just for the record. We needed to find a way. There was no other option.” Chris had seemingly found his voice, cut in harshly. “It was reckless, Jill. We had no clue what would be down there.” They stared each other down. Eventually, Jill smiled thinly. “Well. Leon was.” Chris clicked his tongue in annoyance. “Yeah, your luck. Purest chance if anything.” She could see that Jill was preparing herself for a sharp retort when she quietly stepped in. She didn’t know why. She hadn’t said anything so far. Either way it seemed like everyone around her suddenly were holding their breaths.

“Huh, what a coincidence that you mention sewers.” For an instant she let her gaze wander between them, noticing their distinct irritation. She suppressed a smile, kept her expression entirely blank as she elaborated. “Leon only very recently advertised me to our neighbor as a sewer specialist.” Everyone looked at her, entirely lost. It made her smile after all, as she turned to Jill. “It made me think that maybe the next time you intend to inspect wastewater systems, you could call me. Also, I happen to be what I assume people would call a decent enough shot. Perhaps I’d make an acceptable backup.”

For a moment there was silence. Then Jill simply nodded. She didn’t laugh, didn’t smile. Because she knew had understood exactly what she had said underneath. And she could tell that she was at least considering it. Across from her Chris had fallen into silent staring, his mouth slightly agape. But his expression wasn’t tight. Actually, it looked the lightest it had all evening.

Helena broke the silence. “I have no sullage reference to offer but about the shot… I might be able to confirm this.” She looked her deep in the eye as if asking whether she had crossed a line with that. But she kept her expression entirely relaxed, smiled a little even. “Sounds like not too bad an option then” Claire concluded. “And that way, you at least won’t have to let yourself get irritated by braggy stunt moves and wasted humor next time.” It cracked them and they all laughed. She purposedly avoided it. Even though she admittedly found it a bit challenging. She hid it behind a sly smile, added “Well. Sometimes the jokes aren’t even that bad.” And the laughter only intensified.

When she looked back up, she found Claires eyes all soft. “Sorry, but we'd still need to know what specifically qualifies you for the sewers. Unfortunately, we cannot proceed without being provided with a relevant track record here.” She chuckled, told them about their neighbor Christina and her blocked sink, about having to deal with a plumbing disaster as a result of his coming across immensely capable while working motorcycles in garages. Everyone was listening intently, smiles lingering on their faces and she wondered if it even was the story that held their attention or the small glimpse it had offered into a life. A life behind closed doors. Leon’s. When nothing was burning, when there was no outbreak and no monsters out for a kill.

His eyes found her the moment he let them wander. It was like gravity. He had realized it ever since she had arrived. The awareness within him. He had unconsciously or consciously kept track of her throughout the evening. And, somehow not entirely surprising despite her at least slightly difficult history, he had rarely found her alone.

It was this aura of hers, the mystery, the way she spoke without ever giving herself away. She drew people in without even having to try. Heads turned whenever she walked. Or when she just stood somewhere. Like right now. A group of men had long gathered in her orbit. They all had drinks in their hands, each of them occasionally glanced at her. He didn’t know if she didn’t notice it or chose to simply ignore them, assumed it had to be the latter. It was rather obvious.

And usually, it would have made him jealous, would have provoked him somehow, would have urged him to go over and make it clear who she belonged to. But he realized that currently it didn’t bother him at all. On the contrary. He felt at ease. Certain. This woman was his. And he didn’t need to demonstrate that anymore.

He watched Sherry approach her out from nowhere with something that looked like a soda in her hand, her shoulder nudging hard into a man who stood particularly close and couldn’t seem to take his eyes off Ada and for a second he saw their gazes meet, saw the look Sherry gave him. The guy retreated and he couldn’t help but chuckle. All confidence aside it still felt good to have someone make his point. God how he loved this girl. Ada received the drink in mild amusement and Sherry said something to her ear and then nodded towards Jill and Claire.

And he decided to go over after all.

The evening had been nice. It had felt good. It had felt like… Like everything was in order. Like there was nothing that needed to be fixed. No solution that needed to be found. For anything. For once. No calls. No emergencies. Just a little piece of normality between people who knew shit about things like these.

But he wanted for his woman to take him home. This wasn’t where he wanted to be any longer. He wanted to be with her. Just with her.

She spotted him in the crowd as he approached and didn’t leave him out of her sight afterwards. Her eyes were like an aim, sharp and inescapable. He took note of people looking at him when he stepped into her space, close enough for their thighs to touch. He turned his back on them and leaned towards her, lips brushing her ear without kissing her. But he thought about it. Very thoroughly. “Take me home” he breathed and straightened again to look at her, thought he’d seen her stealing a glance at his lips. But of course she didn’t give in to it, of course her expression left him with nothing. It made him smile faintly.

“Already, Birthday Boy?” She held his gaze. He nodded captivated by her stare. “I want to be with you.” A gentle smile touched her lips, guarded though. “You don’t have to decide. I… am not going anywhere. We have all the time in the world.” We.. His heartrate picked up. “Please.” Her brows lifted as she surveyed him, searching his eyes. “Okay. Lead the way.”

He didn’t. Didn’t move. Just looked at her. Just looked at her in this moment where he felt like he had everything he had ever wanted and more.

She watched them from the distance. His massive back hid most parts of her from her view. But somehow, she could still picture the way they looked at each other right now. She still remembered the moment with them in his living room vividly. And as far as she could tell, not much had changed since then. There was still this quiet understanding between them that had struck her from the very first second.

They barely seemed to need to speak to each other in order to align. They hadn’t needed to sit beside each other, hadn’t needed to touch in order to feel connected.

Their bond was just there. Invisible, but omnipresent. A thread that seemed to remain taut no matter the space between them. She had no idea why she had left him two years ago, what had happened between them since, but it filled her with a deep satisfaction to see now that they had found each other again. And this time, apparently for good.

After a moment he glanced to the side, seeming to regard the others who had ended up scattered in smaller groups or pairs all over the place. Then he looked back at Ada. Her eyes had followed him and after a brief pause, he started walking towards the entrance. And she pursued suit.

She didn’t know who drew more attention, him or her. But they were certainly quite a sight. Each individually, maybe even more so together as a couple. To most people, presumably, because of their looks. They were both attractive, well-shaped. For her, however, it was something else. She had seen this man, a DSO Special Agent with unparalleled experience in the field kneeling beside this woman, falling apart. She had seen the hardness peeling off him layer by layer.
